# P8X Code Review — full source audit

_Fresh-eyes, per-file review of every hand-written C and assembler source file (68 files). Each file was reviewed independently by a reviewer with the P8X ISA / C-subset spec but no prior project context. Grades: A (excellent) … F (poor). Severities are the reviewers' own; ISA-micro-behavior-dependent items were required to be marked low/unverified._

## Executive summary

- **Coverage:** 68/68 files (31 asm, 37 C).
- **Code-quality grades:** A×19, B×45, C×4, D×0, F×0.
- **Documentation grades:** A×54, B×14, C×0, D×0, F×0.
- **Findings:** 6 high, 62 medium, 281 low.

### High-severity findings (fix first)

- **`os/commands-asm/grep.asm`** — cn_a (line 882, LDB #32): The cn[] per-depth stride is computed as 288 bytes (INC ft+1 = +256 at lines 878-880, plus LDB #32/ADD = +32), but cn is declared cn[8][24][16] (line 982, .fill 3072) so the correct per-depth stride is 24*16 = 384. The C source confirms intent: collect() uses a per-frame local cn[384]. clba_a (the sibling helper) correctly strides 48/depth with no spurious INC. Result: for w_depth>=1, cn writes land 96 bytes low per level and overlap the parent depth's name table (depth-1 region [288,672) clobbers depth-0 cn[0][18..23]). During a -r walk of a tree whose subdirectories themselves contain subdirectories, subdir names for indices >=19 at an outer level get corrupted, so cur[]/the recorded rfiles path is wrong and those files are silently skipped or mislabeled.  
  *Fix:* Change LDB #32 to LDB #128 at line 882 (128 low + 256 high = 384). Verify against grep.c's cn[384] per-frame size and add a test with a directory that has >=19 subdirs one of which is itself nested.
- **`os/commands-asm/grep.asm`** — grep_stream overflow guard (lines 295-312) and decl line 975: line[] is declared .fill 176 but the scanner only stops storing when gn >= 255 (LDB #255; CMP; JC gst_l), and gst_eol/gst_end NUL-terminate at line[gn]. Any input line longer than 175 chars writes line[176..255], overrunning into nm[], cur[], and the walk tables. This mirrors grep.c (char line[176] with `if (n < 255)`), so the defect exists in both and the two must stay in sync.  
  *Fix:* Either enlarge line[] to 256 bytes, or change the cap to 175 (LDB #175) so gn never exceeds the buffer. Apply the equivalent fix in os/commands/grep.c to keep the C and asm builds byte-consistent.
- **`os/commands/grep.c`** — grep_stream, line 61 (buffer decl line 27; also lines 50, 66): line[] is 176 bytes but the fill guard is `if (n < 255)`, so n can grow to 255. line[n]=c writes indices 176..254 and the terminator line[n]=0 can write line[255] — up to ~80 bytes past the end of the 176-byte buffer. Any input line longer than 175 chars corrupts whatever global follows line[] (here cur[]/nm[]/rfiles[]). This is a real overrun, not theoretical: log files and source lines routinely exceed 175 chars.  
  *Fix:* Make the guard match the buffer: `if (n < 175)` (leaving room for the NUL at line[175]), or enlarge the buffer to `char line[256];`. Then fix the line-22 header comment to state the true cap.
- **`os/commands/mv.c`** — main glob loop, lines 113-123: The glob-move path never checks whether the computed destination jdst equals the source src. The single-file path guards this with streq (line 102) precisely to avoid deleting the only copy, but the glob path has no equivalent guard. If the destination directory is the same directory the matched files live in (e.g. `MV * .` or `MV *.TMP .` where dst resolves to CWD), jdst == src: move_one does FOPEN(src), FWOPEN(dst) truncates that same file, FGETB immediately hits EOF, an empty file is committed, then FDELETE removes it — the file is destroyed.  
  *Fix:* Inside the loop, after computing jdst, call streq(src, jdst) and skip (or report) that entry instead of calling move_one, mirroring the single-file guard on line 102.
- **`os/commands/vi.c`** — main, '/' and 'n' handlers (lines 425, 431); search()/findfrom() lines 347-350, 333-346: search() sets cy/cx to a match anywhere in the buffer but neither the '/' nor the 'n' handler calls scroll() before redraw(). If the match is below top+22 (or above top after a wrap), redraw() still paints rows top..top+22 with the old top, the matched line is never shown, and placecur() computes gotoxy(cy-top+1, ...) with a row of 24+ — the cursor lands on the status line or off-screen. Searching to any off-screen match is broken.  
  *Fix:* After a successful search, call scroll() (which recenters top) before redraw(), exactly as the G/j/k and undo handlers already do: e.g. `if (search()) { scroll(); redraw(); }` in both the '/' and 'n' branches.
- **`apps/p8xcc.asm`** — st_decl, lines ~2895-2906 (sd_chkeq -> sd_arr): A local scalar declaration with an initializer (int x = 5; / char c = 'a';) falls through into the array-declaration code. After the '=' branch does ADVANCE/GEXPR/EMITSTV (token now ';'), there is no jump past sd_arr, so control drops into sd_arr: JSR ADVANCE which consumes the ';', treats the next token's CURV as an array size (corrupting NLSLOT), marks the var as an array, and ADVANCEs again — eating the next statement's first token. Miscompiles/derails parsing for any local initialized declaration.  
  *Fix:* Insert JMP sd_semi after EMITSTV (line 2905) so the initialized-scalar path terminates instead of falling into sd_arr.

### Medium-severity findings

- **`compiler/p8lib.c`** (correctness) — getline (line 45, 50): The loop guard is `n < max - 1` and the terminator write is `buf[n] = 0`. This is only safe for max >= 1. If a caller passes max == 0 (or negative), `max - 1` is -1; the loop is skipped but `buf[0] = 0` still writes one byte into a zero-length buffer. If int is unsigned in the subset, max==0 makes `max-1` wrap to 65535 and the loop will happily overrun the buffer until CR/LF/EOF.
- **`apps/p8xedit.asm`** (safety) — GETLINE (lines 393-433): No bound on line length. LBUF is one page ($BE00-$BEFF) and the variable block begins immediately at $BF00 (TENDL). Storing (P2)+ has no cap, so a line of 256+ characters walks P2 into $BF00 and the CR handler's STA (P2) (line 432) then writes the null terminator over TENDL/editor state. A pasted or long line silently corrupts the buffer-end pointer.
- **`apps/p8xedit.asm`** (correctness) — LOADFILE (lines 338-351): FLOADAT reads FLEN bytes into TBUF with no check that FLEN <= the 12KB ceiling ($C000..$F000). A file larger than 12KB overruns TBUF past $F000 into whatever lives above, and TEND is then set beyond the ceiling, so subsequent ROOMQ/CHKINS math operates on an already-invalid buffer.
- **`os/commands-asm/awk.asm`** (efficiency) — pfield, lines 621-627: Dead code: the first address computation (LDA pf_st; LDB pf_j; ADD; LDB #<line; ADD; TAP1L; LDA #>line) is completely overwritten by the recomputation at lines 629-640 before P1 is ever dereferenced. It is also wrong on its own (no carry into the high byte), so it only looks correct because the block below replaces it. Executes once per character printed.
- **`os/commands-asm/awk.asm`** (clarity) — a_prog (lines 96-99) and a_pdone (line 146): The 16-bit variable `pp` is written but never read. Lines 96-99 initialize pp to the prog[] pointer, but the copy loop uses (P2) as source and recomputes prog+pl inside prog_put, so pp is unused. Line 146 (`LDA #0; STA pp`) then clobbers it for no effect, and its comment claims pp is 'used as scratch below' which is untrue.
- **`os/commands-asm/cmp.asm`** (safety) — abspath / ab_cp (lines 297-305, 405-466): The copy into the 80-byte `path` buffer (SYS_GETCWD prefix + '/' + relative token) has no length bound. `path: .fill 80` sits immediately before `b1`, so a deep CWD plus a long filename can overflow past 80 bytes and corrupt the start of the file-1 buffer (or the SYS_GETCWD copy itself can overrun).
- **`compiler/p8cc.c`** (correctness) — emit_lt (768), emit_divmod (683), emit_shr (743): All comparisons, division/modulo, and right-shift are unsigned (CMP/JC borrow semantics, SHR zero-fill), yet the compiler produces and stores signed values: unary minus (unary(), line 1046), negative global initializers (toplevel, line 1591). So `if (x < 0)`, `-6 / 2`, and `neg >> 1` all compute wrong results with no diagnostic.
- **`compiler/p8cc.c`** (safety) — whole file: gpool/goff[64], spool/soff[64], vpool/vnoff[64], mac_names/mac_vals[64], stpool[16], mpool/[160], tname[64], tstr[256], nm/dn/tag/pn[64]: No fixed-size table or scratch buffer is ever bounds-checked. addglobal/addvar/intern/mac_define/register_struct and the string-literal path in factor() all write past their arrays once the (low) limits are exceeded — e.g. the 65th string literal, the 65th global, a 64-char identifier, or a 256-byte string literal silently overwrites adjacent globals. Overflow is undetectable and corrupts codegen 
- **`compiler/p8cc.c`** (correctness) — toplevel, lines 1570-1578 (frame reservation): The prologue reserves locals with `LDB #nl` — an 8-bit immediate — then does only a single borrow into the high byte. If a function's total locals exceed 255 bytes (e.g. `char buf[300];`), nl's high bits are dropped: emitdec(nl) prints e.g. 300 but only the low byte is subtracted from __csp, so the frame is under-reserved and locals collide with the caller's frame. No check or diagnostic.
- **`basic/p8xbasic.asm`** (correctness) — DONEXT nx_loop/nx_done, lines 1196-1211: NEXT computes var=var+step then always terminates when var>limit (signed), regardless of the sign of STEP. A countdown loop such as FOR I=10 TO 1 STEP -1 terminates after the first iteration (9 > 1 -> nx_done), so negative STEP is broken even though STEP is an advertised feature (HELP text, line 4118).
- **`basic/p8xbasic.asm`** (safety) — DOGOSUB ~950, DOFOR fp_alloc ~1077, VARFIND vf_new ~1878, SVARFIND svf2new ~2644: None of the fixed tables are bounds-checked before appending. A 4th GOSUB (GSTK holds 3), 3rd FOR (FSTK holds 2), 33rd variable (NVARS=32), or 17th string var (NSVARS=16) writes past the table into adjacent scratch/state — silent memory corruption rather than a diagnostic. E.g. GSTK+GSP*4 with GSP=3 lands on GSP itself; VARTAB+i*8 with i=32 overflows the low byte back to VARTAB[0].
- **`basic/p8xbasic.asm`** (efficiency) — STMT 244-338; also FACTOR 1511+, SPEEK 2503+, STERM 2912+, EVAL 1248+, CKLEAD 3742+: Each dispatcher reloads the test byte with LDA (P2) before every LDB #TOK / CMP / JZ triple. Per the ISA, CMP preserves A and LDB writes only B, so A already holds the char across the whole chain — every reload after the first is dead work. STMT alone does ~20 redundant memory reads per statement dispatch; the pattern repeats in six routines and inflates code size noticeably.
- **`apps/p8xasm.asm`** (correctness) — SYMDEFVAL, lines 702-705: The table-full guard only compares the HIGH byte of SYMP against >SYMEND ($C0): 'LDA SYMP+1; LDB #>SYMEND; CMP; JC SD_FULL'. Any entry whose start is anywhere in page $BF passes the check, but a 14-byte entry starting as high as $BFFE writes through $C00B — 12 bytes past SYMEND ($C000) into the variable block (SRCPATH etc.). I confirmed the 1098th symbol (index 1097) starts at $BFFE and overflows 
- **`os/commands-asm/diff.asm`** (efficiency) — leq / le_l loop (408-443), laddr calls at 418 & 429: laddr recomputes base + line*80 (via a repeated-add multiply loop that iterates line-index times) for BOTH lines on EVERY byte comparison. Comparing line N costs ~N add-iterations per byte, twice — quadratic in line index. loadlines (ll_rd, line 353) has the same shape, recomputing lln*80 per character.
- **`os/commands-asm/find.asm`** (safety) — walk (w_depth increment, lines 334-340) / array sizing lines 734-738: There is no runtime guard preventing w_depth from reaching/exceeding MAXD (10). On a directory tree deeper than 10 levels, w_depth++ keeps going and the index helpers (nsub_a/idx_a/parr_a/clba_a/cn_a) compute addresses past the ends of nsub/idx/parr/clba/cn, silently corrupting adjacent TPA memory. The header comment even acknowledges this ('guarding recursion depth is the caller's dir-tree shape'
- **`os/commands-asm/find.asm`** (docs) — print_match header, line 368: Comment claims SYS_PUTC 'clobbers P1/P2 via syscall', but pm_l (376-386) and pm_nl (402-408) rely on P1 surviving across SYS_PUTC. If the comment were accurate the command would print garbage after the first character; since it evidently works, the comment is wrong. A future maintainer trusting it could 'fix' working code or wrongly reload P1 each iteration.
- **`os/commands-asm/dir.asm`** (correctness) — walk (w_depth increment ~line 463-465; nsub_addr/idx_addr/csub_addr 1428-1494): Recursion depth is never bounded. nsub[]/idx[] are 16 bytes and csub[] is 2048 bytes = exactly 16 levels x 128. At w_depth==16 (a directory tree 17 levels deep) nsub_addr/idx_addr index one past their arrays (into the neighboring array) and csub_addr computes offset 16*128=2048, one past csub[] — silent memory corruption, not a clean error.
- **`os/commands-asm/dir.asm`** (efficiency) — putnum (1181-1230), ndigits (1235-1259); storage pn/pncnt/ndv/ndc (1607-1610): putnum and ndigits are dead code — no JSR targets them anywhere; the live size column uses the 24-bit putsize/dm10 path. They plus their scratch (pn, pncnt, ndv, ndc) consume code+data bytes in the ~37.9K TPA for no benefit.
- **`os/commands-asm/cp.asm`** (safety) — copy_tree recursion, lines 666-668 (w_depth INC): Recursion depth (w_depth) is incremented before each recursive JSR copy_tree with no upper-bound check. The per-depth arrays hold exactly 8 levels (sp/dp = 8*80, names = 8*288, isd = 8*24, nn/cidx = 8). A directory tree deeper than 8 levels drives w_depth to 8+, so spd_a computes sp+8*80 = the start of dp, names_a/isd_a overflow into neighbouring arrays, and the copy silently corrupts adjacent scr
- **`os/commands-asm/cp.asm`** (correctness) — ct_tl / ct_padl, lines 557-591: Directory-entry names are copied into fixed 12-byte slots. The copy stops only on a space (32) or when ctk reaches 12; NUL-termination happens only in the ct_padl (space-found) path. A dirent name that fills all 12 bytes with no trailing space (the type byte is at de+12, so the name field is de[0..11] = 12 bytes) leaves the slot unterminated, so joinp's name-copy loop (which reads until NUL) runs 
- **`os/commands-asm/disasm.asm`** (docs) — PRS, lines 360-361: The routine header states PRS uses P2 as the cursor save and re-points P2 each character. The implementation instead saves the pointer in the PSL/PSH globals (TPA1L->PSL, TPA1H->PSH) and reconstructs P1 from them; P2 is never read or written. The comment misdescribes register liveness, which is exactly the kind of thing a maintainer relies on when reasoning about clobbers.
- **`os/commands-asm/disasm.asm`** (efficiency) — CALC_LN (180-191), DS_OPER (206-224), do_preg (276-282), HEXVAL (469-490): The ISA guarantees CMP preserves A, and LDB does not touch A, so after 'LDA SHAPE / LDB #n / CMP' the accumulator still holds SHAPE. Yet the code reloads 'LDA SHAPE' before every subsequent compare (3x in CALC_LN, ~4x in DS_OPER, 2x in do_preg), and similarly reloads 'LDA HVT' in HEXVAL where A is already the folded value. Each redundant load is wasted bytes and cycles in hot paths.
- **`os/commands-asm/man.asm`** (efficiency) — m_build (lines 45-60) and mb_l (lines 71-73): The prefix build writes '/man/' as five (STA (P1)) + (INP1) pairs, and the copy loop does STA (P1)/INP1/INP2 separately. The ISA and the rest of this codebase (cp.asm, diff.asm, dir.asm, cmp.asm all use STA (P1)+) support post-increment stores, so every explicit INP1 here is a wasted instruction.
- **`firmware/p8xmon.asm`** (safety) — GETLINE (GL1 loop, ~line 1790-1807): The input line loop stores each echoed character with STA (P2)+ and has no check that P2 has reached the end of the 64-byte line buffer (LBUF=$6000, next variable ADDRL=$6040). A line longer than 63 characters overruns LBUF and corrupts the monitor/FS scratch variables (ADDRL/ADDRH/HEXL/HEXH and beyond), and a very long line eventually walks into the sector-buffer region.
- **`os/commands-asm/mv.asm`** (correctness) — mv_glob / mg_l loop (lines 196-262): The glob path has no same-file guard (streq is only applied in the single-source path at lines 170-181). A command like `MV *.TMP .` resolves each match's src to /cwd/NAME and joinp builds jdst = /cwd/NAME — the identical file. move_one then FOPENs src, FWOPEN truncates the same file to empty, the copy loop reads nothing, and FDELETE removes it: silent data loss. This flaw is also present in mv.c,
- **`os/commands-asm/mv.asm`** (safety) — mpw_l (79-104), abspath ab_copy (530-545), joinp (357-406), SYS_GETCWD path in abspath: No bounds checking on any fixed buffer. patw/m_src/m_dst/m_jdst are 80 bytes; the source-word copy, abspath output, GETCWD result, and joinp concatenation (dst up to 80 + '/' + basename up to 64) can all overrun. Since these buffers are laid out contiguously at the end of the file (patw, m_src, m_dst, m_jdst, gfiles), an overflow silently corrupts the adjacent buffer. Matches the C version's lack 
- **`os/commands-asm/grep.asm`** (safety) — collect / w_depth increment (lines 580-586): w_depth is incremented before each recursive collect() with no bound check, but the per-depth tables are sized for exactly 8 levels (nsub/gidxr/parr .fill 8; clba .fill 384=8*48; cn .fill 3072=8*384). A directory tree deeper than 8 levels indexes past every table (nsub_a/idx_a/parr_a/clba_a/cn_a all add w_depth unclamped) and corrupts adjacent RAM; deep JSR recursion also grows the P3 stack unboun
- **`os/commands-asm/grep.asm`** (safety) — append_file (lines 610-714) / rfiles decl line 983: rfiles[] slots are a fixed 80 bytes (rf_addr multiplies rfi*80), but the assembled path is cur[0..fpl) + '/' + nm + NUL, and cur[] is 176 bytes. A collected path longer than 79 chars overruns its slot into the next entry (or past rfiles for the last slot). No length check bounds the copy. Shared with grep.c (base = nrf*80).
- **`os/commands-asm/grep.asm`** (docs) — do_match header (line 353): Comment asserts 'match/SYS_PUTC clobber P1/P2', but SYS_PUTC ($2009 -> OUTCH) provably preserves P1 and P2 on all three paths (OPUTS at os/p8xos.asm:4630 relies on it, and OUTFILE saves/restores P1, the capture path saves/restores P2). The dmp_l loop (lines 376-386) itself depends on P1 surviving SYS_PUTC. The comment is false for SYS_PUTC and could lead a maintainer to 'fix' the working loop or t
- **`os/commands/cat.c`** (safety) — catpath, lines 42-51: The absolute path is assembled into fixed `char path[80]` with no bounds check. The CWD (from SYS_GETCWD) is copied, a '/' may be appended, then the argument is appended char-by-char — none of these guard against i reaching 80. A deep CWD plus a long filename overruns path[], corrupting adjacent globals. This is worst in the glob path: each gbuf slot is up to 64 bytes and catpath prepends the CWD 
- **`os/commands/cmp.c`** (safety) — main lines 75, 92 (abspath into path[]): abspath(path, a) writes the CWD-prefixed path into the fixed 80-byte global path[] with no length bound. A long relative argument (CWD prefix + arg name > 79 chars) would overrun path[] and corrupt the adjacent globals (b1/n1). Whether this can actually happen depends on abspath's own internal clamping, which is not visible in this file.
- **`os/commands-asm/sort.asm`** (efficiency) — laddr (274-321): Slot base = lines + row*80 is computed by adding 80 in a loop that runs `row` times (up to 127 iterations of a 16-bit add). laddr is the hottest routine in the file — called twice per byte in the swap loop and twice per char in lless — so this repeated-addition multiply dominates total run time on the TTL CPU.
- **`os/commands-asm/sort.asm`** (efficiency) — so_swap / sw_l (162-217): The 80-byte slot swap calls laddr twice for every column (160 laddr calls per swapped pair), even though the two base addresses are constant across the k-loop and the bytes are contiguous.
- **`os/commands-asm/sort.asm`** (efficiency) — selection sort so_swap (161-221): Sorting physically copies 80-byte slot contents on every swap. Selection sort does up to nline-1 swaps, each moving 80 bytes through the expensive laddr path — O(n^2) comparisons plus O(n*80) byte copies with per-byte address recomputation.
- **`os/commands/cp.c`** (safety) — main, lines 164-169: The SRC-word copy loop `while (a[i] != 0 && a[i] != 13 && a[i] != 32) { ... patw[i] = a[i]; i = i + 1; }` has no upper bound on i, and patw is only 80 bytes. A source word of 80+ characters overruns patw[] (and the trailing `patw[i]=0` writes at index 80 for an exactly-80-char word), corrupting adjacent globals (gfiles/src/dst).
- **`os/commands/cp.c`** (safety) — joinp, lines 52-61 (called at 130-131, 195): joinp() writes dir + '/' + name into out with no length check. out is jsrc/jdst[80]. During deep -r recursion sp accumulates one '/name' segment per level; once <sp>/<name> exceeds 80 bytes the write overruns jsrc/jdst, and the subsequent scopy(sp, sp0) into the child's 80-byte sp overruns too. Not reachable on shallow trees but unbounded by depth.
- **`os/commands/cp.c`** (correctness) — copy_tree, lines 117-121: The name-trim copies de[] bytes until the first 0x20 pad or k==12, then NUL-fills only while k<12. If a directory entry name occupies all 12 bytes with no space pad, no NUL terminator is written in the slot, so joinp()'s `while (name[j]!=0)` reads past the 12-byte slot into the next entry (or past names[288] for the last slot). Depends on the on-disk max filename length; if names are guaranteed <=
- **`os/commands/disasm.c`** (correctness) — main, lines 92-98 (found sentinel): The value 99 is overloaded as both the 'not found' marker and a real table index. found is set to i on a match; if a matching opcode ever sits at index 99, `if (found == 99)` misclassifies it as unknown and prints '???' plus advances only 1 byte, corrupting the rest of the disassembly. This is dormant only because lib_distab.c currently has dt_n = 88. The opcode is a full 8-bit byte and the table 
- **`os/commands/dir.c`** (safety) — main, lines 339 and 361 (gpat copy): The glob pattern / file leaf is copied from the user's argument into gpat[16] with no bounds check: `while (ls < i) { gpat[j] = arg[ls]; j++; ls++; }` and the analogous file-mode loop. Although on-disk names are max 12 chars, gpat holds a user-typed pattern, which can be arbitrarily long (e.g. `DIR *ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRST`). Writing past gpat[15] overruns into the adjacent global (enam[]), corruptin
- **`os/commands/dir.c`** (correctness) — collect, lines 207-217 (the `if (k < 64)` cap): Entries beyond 64 in a single directory are silently dropped, and walk()'s sub[64] similarly caps recorded subdirectories at 64. Per project memory, P8X directories can span multiple extents / grow past one LBA, so a directory with >64 entries will list an incomplete result with no indication to the user — a real functional defect for a listing command, worse than an error.
- **`os/commands/head.c`** (correctness) — lines 28-34 (the `-` branch): After seeing '-', n is reset to 0 and digits are parsed, but there is no check that at least one digit was consumed. An unrecognized flag like `head -x file` (or any `-` not followed by a digit and not `-h`/`-H`) falls into this branch, parses zero digits, leaves n=0, and then the copy loop `lines < n` is `0 < 0` immediately false — so the command silently prints nothing and exits 0. The user gets
- **`os/commands/find.c`** (safety) — walk, lines 107-110: The accumulated path cur[256] is grown on every descent (append '/' + child name) with NO bounds check on plen. Each level adds up to 13 chars ('/' + 12-char name), so a tree deeper than ~19 levels, or fewer levels with long names, writes past cur[255] and corrupts adjacent globals (nm/pat/isglob) or beyond. cur[plen]=0 at line 110 can also write at index 256 (one past the array) when plen==256. N
- **`os/commands/grep.c`** (safety) — collect, lines 119-127 (buffer decl line 30): rfiles[] uses an 80-byte stride (base = nrf*80) but the code writes plen bytes of cur, a separator, then the name and a NUL with no check that this fits in 80 bytes. Absolute paths in a deep tree easily exceed 79 chars; the write runs into the next slot, and for the final slot (nrf=35, base=2800) past the end of rfiles[2880]. Silent corruption.
- **`os/commands/grep.c`** (safety) — collect lines 149-152 and main lines 193-195: cur[] is 176 bytes and is extended in place during recursion (cur[plen]='/' then each child-name char) with no bound on plen; SYS_GETCWD at line 193 also writes cur with no length cap. A deep directory tree or long CWD overflows cur[].
- **`os/commands/lib_dirent.c`** (correctness) — de_isdot (line 43): Only de[0] is tested, so the predicate returns true for ANY entry whose name begins with '.', not just the "." and ".." directory entries. If the P8X filesystem permits leading-dot filenames (dotfiles), a listing loop that uses de_isdot() to skip . and .. would also silently hide those legitimate entries. The comment acknowledges the loose match but frames it as intended.
- **`os/commands-asm/tail.asm`** (clarity) — t_nl (168-194) vs t_feof (197-224): The 'close current line (write NUL) + advance slot with (slot+1)%n wrap + increment 16-bit total' sequence is duplicated almost verbatim in two places. A future change to ring-advance or the terminator must be made in both, and the two copies can silently drift.
- **`os/commands/lib_globx.c`** (safety) — line 71 (leaf copy loop): The leaf-component copy `while (ls < i) { leaf[j] = pat[ls]; ... }` has no bound against leaf's size (leaf[16]). A pattern whose last component exceeds 15 characters overruns leaf and its NUL at leaf[j]. Nothing in glob_expand enforces the P8X 12-char name limit on the typed pattern, so a long literal-plus-wildcard leaf corrupts adjacent stack/globals.
- **`os/commands/lib_globx.c`** (safety) — lines 88-93 (out slot assembly): The output path is assembled as dir prefix + name + NUL into a fixed 64-byte slot with no check that the total fits. dir may be up to 63 chars and name up to 12, so a deep named-directory prefix can push the written path past 64 bytes, corrupting the next slot (or, for the final slot, memory past `out`). Only the match COUNT is bounded (cnt < maxn), never the per-slot LENGTH.
- **`os/commands/lib_regex.c`** (correctness) — matchhere (whole file); * + ? loops: Quantifiers are non-greedy: each loop tries the tail (matchhere(re+2,...)) BEFORE consuming another character, so the FIRST tail match wins and the match ends as early as possible. For grep (yes/no) this is invisible, but for sed's s/// the matched span (rend - start) is the shortest one, so e.g. s/a+/X/ on "aaa" replaces a single 'a', and s/<.*>/X/ stops at the first '>'. This contradicts the gre
- **`os/commands/lib_stdin.c`** (safety) — open_path, lines 55-58: The file word is copied into path[80] on top of the CWD prefix with no bounds check. A deep CWD plus a long name (or a long absolute argument / glob entry) writes past path[79], corrupting the adjacent globals fromfile/gfiles/gnf/gidx and beyond. The comment on lines 53-54 acknowledges the assumption but the code never enforces it, so malformed or long input silently corrupts memory rather than fa
- **`os/commands/lib_stdin.c`** (correctness) — nextc, line 82: open_path(p)'s return value is discarded; the comment asserts 'a match always exists -> opens'. That holds only at glob time — if a matched file is deleted or becomes unreadable between glob_expand and the roll-forward, open_path returns 2 (FOPEN failed) and the subsequent bios(FGETB) on line 83 reads from an unopened/stale stream. Behavior then depends on BIOS FGETB semantics for a failed open (u
- **`os/commands/man.c`** (safety) — name-copy loop, lines 49-51: The loop copies the argument into the fixed global path[80] starting at index 5 with no upper-bound check on i. An argument longer than 74 characters overruns the buffer and corrupts the adjacent global(s), and the NUL write at line 52 lands even further out. The argument comes from the command tail (argstr()), i.e. untrusted user input, so this is reachable.
- **`os/commands/mv.c`** (correctness) — move_one, lines 54-64: The return value of FWOPEN is not checked. If creating/truncating DST fails (destination directory does not exist, disk full, name invalid), move_one still runs the copy loop, FCLOSE, and then unconditionally FRESOLVE+FDELETE on SRC. A failed copy therefore still deletes the source — data loss. This is most reachable on the single-file path, where dst is an arbitrary user-supplied path whose paren
- **`os/commands/mv.c`** (safety) — main, lines 89-93: The SRC word is copied into patw[80] with no bound on i; the while loop copies until whitespace/CR/NUL. A source argument of 80+ non-space characters overruns patw and writes into the adjacent global (gfiles). The same unchecked pattern feeds abspath into src[80]/dst[80].
- **`os/commands/touch.c`** (correctness) — exists() (lines 29-33): On the file-exists path, FOPEN succeeds but the function returns 1 without ever calling FCLOSE, so the just-opened read stream is left open. Every already-existing name passed to TOUCH leaks/keeps an open handle; touching many existing files (e.g. `TOUCH A B C D ...`) could exhaust stream slots or leave stale FS state for later commands.
- **`os/commands/tail.c`** (correctness) — line 64 / 75 (total counter): `total` is a signed 16-bit int incremented once per line. On input with >32767 lines it overflows to negative. The print phase then computes count=total (negative) and `count > n` is false, so count stays negative and the `while (count > 0)` loop never runs — tail silently prints nothing instead of the last N lines. `base` is also left at 0 since `total > n` is false.
- **`os/commands-asm/vi.asm`** (correctness) — nm_l (lines 225-244), interacting with inschar: On an empty line (llen==0), 'l' computes nm_t = len-1 = 0-1 = 255 (borrow), then CMP cx(0) vs 255 clears carry, so JNC nm_lok is taken and cx is advanced to 1 — past the end of a 0-length line. This is the only path that makes cx>len (non-empty lines correctly stop at len-1). If the user then enters insert and types, inschar runs with cx=1 > len=0: its shift loop falls straight through to ic_set, 
- **`os/commands-asm/vi.asm`** (efficiency) — laddr (605-652) called inside copyline (1119-1144), inschar (1028-1052), delchar (1086-1113), splitline, opendown, delline, insline: laddr builds va_i*80 by a repeated-+80 loop (up to 109 iterations) every call. copyline calls laddr TWICE per byte (src and dst) inside its per-column loop, so a single line copy is ~2*80 laddr calls, each up to ~109 adds (~17k inner adds); delline/opendown/splitline copy many lines, multiplying this. inschar/delchar shift loops likewise recompute i*80 twice per shifted byte.
- **`os/commands/vi.c`** (correctness) — getpat() line 363, used by '/' handler line 424: On an empty '/' entry (k==0), getpat() unconditionally executes pat[k]=0 -> pat[0]=0, wiping any previously entered pattern. Because havepat stays 1 from a prior search, the '/' handler then calls search() with an empty pat; matchat() returns 1 immediately (pat[0]==0), so the cursor just jumps one char forward instead of repeating the last search. This directly contradicts the line 364 comment 'em
- **`os/p8xos.asm`** (docs) — lines 324, 660/1660, 409-418: Comments repeatedly state the command word (and PATH arg) is 'upcased in PARSEW', but PARSEW preserves case (line 3730) and keywords are lowercase, so matching is case-sensitive-lowercase. The comments contradict actual behavior.
- **`apps/p8xcc.asm`** (correctness) — MCMP_DEF (__cmp) + EMITCMP (ec_lt/ec_ge/ec_gt/ec_le): Relational operators compile to __cmp then JC/JNC — UNSIGNED 16-bit comparison — but C int is signed. if (x < 0), a < b with negatives give wrong results. The ISA has signed BLT/BGE/BLE/BGT, unused. A real semantic gap.
- **`os/commands-asm/tree.asm`** (correctness) — tw_desc / walk recursion (lines 188-191) vs array sizes: No bound check on w_depth before INC w_depth / JSR walk. nsub/idx are .fill 16 and csub is .fill 768 (16 levels); a tree deeper than 16 levels indexes past the arrays (nsub[16] aliases idx[0], csub runs off the end) — silent memory corruption. Real divergence from tree.c, whose per-level locals are naturally stack-bounded.

---

## Per-file reviews

## Firmware / BIOS

### `firmware/p8xmon.asm`  
**Code: B  ·  Docs: B**  ·  P8X ASSEMBLY

ROM monitor for the P8X TTL computer: serial console command loop (examine/modify, dump, CF init/format/boot, go), a stable BIOS jump table at $0100, a bounded CF/ATA sector driver, and a P8XFS v2 filesystem layer (find/create/delete/commit/open/read/write/dir-iterate) shared with the OS.

This is a well-organized, mature piece of firmware. The console/parse helpers, CF driver, and FS routines are cleanly separated, carry-chain arithmetic for multi-byte counters is handled carefully and consistently (the recurring "SUB the 256-unit mid byte, JC to continue, handle borrow into the high byte" idiom is correct in FGETB, FCOMMIT, FLOADAT and FCREATE), and NIBBLE/GETADDR/PRBYTE are textbook-correct given the documented flag semantics. Documentation is a genuine strength: a full file header, per-routine headers, and mostly-accurate inline comments. The main concerns are a missing bounds check in GETLINE (a real overflow), a hardware-dependent drive-select bit that looks wrong for drive 1 (unverified), and a scattering of stale/contradictory comments.

**Findings:**

- _medium_ · safety · GETLINE (GL1 loop, ~line 1790-1807) — The input line loop stores each echoed character with STA (P2)+ and has no check that P2 has reached the end of the 64-byte line buffer (LBUF=$6000, next variable ADDRL=$6040). A line longer than 63 characters overruns LBUF and corrupts the monitor/FS scratch variables (ADDRL/ADDRH/HEXL/HEXH and beyond), and a very long line eventually walks into the sector-buffer region. → Add a length guard: compare P2 (low byte, since the buffer is page-local) against LBUF+63 before storing; on overflow, either drop the character (optionally ring the bell) or force line termination. This also hardens the backspace empty-check at GLBS, which already relies on the buffer never leaving page $60.
- _low_ · isa-misuse · CFINIT (~line 518) and CFSETL (~line 548): LDA #$E0 / LDB DRVSEL / OR — UNVERIFIED (hardware-dependent). DRVSEL (0/1) is ORed into the drive/head register CFHEAD ($E0). In a standard ATA/CF register layout the master/slave DEV bit is bit 4 ($10); bits 3:0 are LBA[27:24] in LBA mode. As written, drive 1 sets bit 0 = LBA[24]=1 rather than selecting a second device, which would either address sector N+16M on the master or, if a second card is decoded externally, still corrupt LBA bit 24 on that card. The comment 'ATA device-select bit' and CFSETL's 'LBA[27:24]=0' cannot both be true. → Confirm against the CF socket schematic. If the second card uses the ATA slave/DEV bit, this should be '$E0 | (DRVSEL<<4)'. If the second card is selected by external address decode driven from DRVSEL, then DRVSEL should NOT be ORed into CFHEAD at all (keep it $E0). I could not confirm the wiring from the source, so flagging low but note the potential impact is total loss of drive-1 access.
- _low_ · correctness · FCRE_CORE data-write loop, FC_WR (~lines 1090-1118) — The remaining-bytes counter for the data copy is loaded only from FLEN (low) and FLEN+1 (mid) into TMP/TMP2 — the 24-bit high byte FLEN+2 is ignored, so the write loop is effectively 16-bit and would under-copy a file whose length exceeds 64KB. The directory entry it later writes (FC_WNM/length store) does record the full 24-bit FLEN+2, so entry length and bytes-actually-written would disagree for >64KB sources. → In practice FSRC is a 16-bit RAM pointer so the source cannot exceed the address space, making this unreachable today — but it is an inconsistency with the 24-bit FS. Either promote FC_WR to a 24-bit remaining counter (matching FCOMMIT/FLOADAT), or add a comment stating FCREATE is intentionally limited to <=64KB in-RAM sources and asserting FLEN+2==0.
- _low_ · docs · CF driver header (~line 458) vs cfw_lp/cfd_lp (~lines 463, 476) — The block header claims the bounded wait is 'up to 65536 status polls', but CFTOH is seeded with $F0, so the counter runs $F000..$FFFF = 4096 iterations. The comment at line 463 correctly says 4096, while the comment at line 476 says '65536 polls elapsed'. The file contradicts itself on the timeout bound. → Pick the real number (4096 as coded) and make the header and the line-476 comment agree, or change the $F0 seed to $00 if 65536 was actually intended.
- _low_ · docs · CF driver header 'absent drive ... times out' vs CFDRQ (~lines 479-495) — For a truly absent device the task file floats to $FF. CFWAIT (BSY=bit7) does spin and time out as claimed, but CFDRQ tests DRQ=bit3: $FF & $08 is non-zero, so CFDRQ returns immediately treating garbage as 'DRQ ready' rather than timing out. The subsequent transfer reads $FF bytes. No hang occurs, but the 'times out' description is inaccurate for the DRQ path. → Reword the comment to 'CFWAIT times out on a stuck-BSY absent drive; CFDRQ returns quickly and the caller reads garbage', or gate reads on the CFWAIT/error-status result before trusting DRQ.
- _low_ · clarity · FS routines throughout (e.g. FSCAN, FCRE_CORE, FDEL_CORE) — Monitor-oriented scratch equates are reused with unrelated meanings in the FS layer: HEXH serves as a name-match flag, HEXL as a byte counter, ADDRL/ADDRH as a directory-sector cursor, CNT as a sector countdown, and FCDH is described as '(HEXL)'. This forces the reader to track that 'HEXH'/'ADDRL' no longer mean what their names and primary equate comments say. → Either add local aliases in memmap.inc (e.g. MATCHF = HEXH, NAMECNT = HEXL) and use those in the FS code, or add a one-line 'scratch reuse' note at the top of each FS routine mapping the borrowed registers to their local role.
- _low_ · style · CMD_E first-key handling (~line 198) — After 'JSR GETC / LDB #CR / CMP', the code does 'LDA TMP' to reload the key before the '.' test. Since CMP preserves A (per the ISA header), A still holds the key and the reload is redundant. (Contrast line 311, where the reload after CRLF is genuinely required because CRLF clobbers A — that one is correct.) → Drop the redundant 'LDA TMP' at line 198, or add a comment clarifying it is defensive; leave line 311 as-is.

**Docs:** Excellent structure: file header documents memory map, ABI stability contract, ISA/flag conventions, and even the two required new microcode opcodes (JMP/JSR (P1)) with cycle-level microcode. Nearly every routine has a header stating inputs, outputs, carry meaning, and clobbers. Deductions: (1) the CFWAIT/CFDRQ block header says "up to 65536 status polls" but the code starts CFTOH at $F0, giving ~4096 polls — and the inline comment at cfw_lp correctly says 4096 while the comment at line 476 again says "65536 polls elapsed", so the file contradicts itself; (2) CFSETL's "LBA[27:24]=0" comment is false whenever DRVSEL=1, since DRVSEL is ORed into CFHEAD bit 0; (3) the CF driver header claims an absent drive "times out", but CFDRQ reads a floating $FF as DRQ-set and returns immediately rather than timing out (benign, but the comment misleads); (4) some equate names (HEXL/HEXH/ADDRL/CNT) are documented for their monitor role but silently reused as FS scratch, so comments like "(16-bit: HEXL/FCDH)" require cross-referencing.

**Strengths:** Multi-byte (16/24-bit) counter arithmetic is handled correctly and consistently across FGETB, FCOMMIT ceil(FLEN/512), FLOADAT and FCREATE, using a single well-understood borrow-chain idiom (subtract 2 from the 256-unit mid byte for -512, JC to continue, propagate borrow into the high byte) — the exact class of code where JC/JNC bugs usually hide, and it is right here.; Carry/flag usage matches the documented semantics precisely: NIBBLE, GETADDR's 16-bit shift-by-4 (SHL on low / ROL on high through carry), the DASC printable-range test, and the C=set/clear return convention on every FS call are all correct.; Clever, space-efficient ISA use: PRBYTE falls through into PRNIB to print both nibbles; GETC leaves a TMP copy so callers can re-test the key after clobbering A; SEC/CLC used deliberately as 1-byte carry returns.; Documentation is unusually thorough for firmware — a full file header with memory map and ABI-stability warning, per-routine headers stating inputs/outputs/carry/clobbers, and even the microcode for the two new opcodes the monitor depends on.; Thoughtful robustness details: bounded CF polling so an absent card can't hang the machine, read/write streams that capture and re-assert their own drive (ROSDRV/WOSDRV) for dual-volume correctness, and FRESOLVE centralizing drive-1 mount handling so no other caller parses a drive prefix.

**Top improvements:** Add a bounds check to GETLINE so a long console line cannot overflow the 64-byte LBUF into the FS scratch variables.; Verify the drive-select bit in CFINIT/CFSETL against the CF wiring and fix it (DRVSEL<<4 for an ATA slave, or remove the OR for externally-decoded cards); today's '$E0 | DRVSEL' sets LBA[24], not the DEV bit, for drive 1.; Reconcile the contradictory documentation (4096 vs 65536 poll bound; CFSETL 'LBA[27:24]=0'; CFDRQ 'times out') so the comments match the code.; Either make FCREATE's data-write loop 24-bit like the rest of the FS, or document and assert its intentional <=64KB in-RAM-source limit to avoid an entry-length/bytes-written mismatch.


## OS kernel

### `os/p8xos.asm`  
**Code: B  ·  Docs: B**  ·  ASM

The RAM-resident P8X/OS kernel: interactive shell with line editor + history + Tab completion, a P8XFS v2 filesystem client, redirection/pipes, a mini make/sh engine, and built-in commands (CD/LOAD/RUN/SAVE/DEL/MKDIR/RMDIR/PACK/FSCK/FORMAT/bootload).

A large, mature, impressively careful piece of hand-written assembly. The hard parts — 16-bit LBA arithmetic, multi-byte subtract/compare with manual borrow chains, tree-walk with an explicit frame stack, the FFIND-before-FWOPEN ordering constraint — are correct and unusually well explained. No clear high-severity logic bug found; the real weaknesses are pervasive code repetition, a few depth-limited walks, and several stale comments that contradict the code (notably the repeated 'upcased' claim).

**Findings:**

- _medium_ · docs · lines 324, 660/1660, 409-418 — Comments repeatedly state the command word (and PATH arg) is 'upcased in PARSEW', but PARSEW preserves case (line 3730) and keywords are lowercase, so matching is case-sensitive-lowercase. The comments contradict actual behavior. → Delete/replace the 'upcased' comments; state that command words are case-sensitive and must be lowercase.
- _low_ · correctness · SYS_DIRENTRY (lines 162-165) — Bytes [15..16] of the snapshot are filled from the global LBA/LBA1 I/O sector registers, which after a BIOS FNEXT hold the last directory sector read, not necessarily the matched entry's start LBA. If the BIOS doesn't leave the entry start LBA there, callers like cp -r that descend via this field break. Unverified — depends on BIOS FNEXT post-state. → Confirm against firmware that LBA/LBA1 equal the entry start LBA after FNEXT; if not, source these from the entry (as FINDENT does).
- _low_ · correctness · FSCK (line 2527), PK2FIND (3007), PK2FIX (3180) — Tree walk / PACK capped at 8 frames (depth>=7 stops descent). Directories nested deeper are silently not checked/compacted/repaired — FSCK could report OK and PACK leave deep extents unmoved with no diagnostic. → Emit a 'tree too deep — not fully checked' note when the depth limit is hit.
- _low_ · efficiency · pervasive (every CFREAD/CFWRITE site) — The 4-line 'STA LBA / LDA #0 / STA LBA1 / STA LBA2' idiom (plus restore LBA1=0) is repeated dozens of times, as are the 16-bit increment and slot-stride (*32) loops — inflating code size against the 16K OS cap and multiplying copy-paste risk. → Factor helpers: SETLBA16, CLRLBAHI, INC16/DEC16, ADVSLOT. Net code shrink, no behavior change.
- _low_ · clarity · ZSB (lines 2244-2261) — Two fully-unrolled 256-iteration zero loops with near-identical bodies, split only to keep the counter 8-bit — bulky and easy to desync if edited. → Single loop over a 16-bit counter, or a note that the halves must stay identical.
- _low_ · clarity · DT_TERM (4125-4126) and de_lp guard (4070-4071) — Room-check comments phrase the comparison operands opposite to the code; the code is correct, the prose is confusing. → Reword comments to match the JC/JNC sense.
- _low_ · clarity · OS RAM variable block (lines 80-107) — Many working-set section headers with no symbols beneath them (equates live in memmap.inc), leaving a long stretch of comment-only scaffolding that reads as dead. → Move the notes next to the real definitions in memmap.inc, or trim the empty headers.

**Docs:** Documentation is a genuine strength: thorough file header (memory map, build steps, command list), a header on essentially every routine (inputs/outputs/clobbers), and excellent inline rationale for subtle bits (LBA1-at-rest invariant, FFIND/FWOPEN ordering, carry decisions). Marked down because several comments are stale and misleading: multiple sites claim the command word / PATH arg is 'upcased' (DISPATCH 324, PARSECOMP 1660, DOPATH 409/418) when PARSEW preserves case and matching is case-sensitive-lowercase — a reader trusting them would expect HELP to work.

**Strengths:** Multi-byte (16/24-bit) arithmetic consistently correct: SAVE length subtract chains borrow properly; every 16-bit magnitude compare uses the high-byte carry correctly, falling to the low compare only when high bytes are equal.; Subtle ordering hazards identified and handled — 'do all FFINDs before FWOPEN because FWOPEN claims SBUF', and '>>' raw-sector prepend via APCOPY so an open stdin read-stream survives.; Register/pointer discipline across BIOS calls is careful: OUTFILE saves/restores P1 around FPUTB with balanced PHA/PLA; DESCEND preserves P2 around FINDENT; OUTCH documented+implemented to preserve P1/P2 so redirection works everywhere.; The 'LBA1=0 at rest' invariant is a clean, explicitly-documented convention preventing 16-bit dir scans from corrupting later absolute/boot-block reads.

**Top improvements:** Fix the stale 'upcased' comments (and the reversed room-check comments) so docs match the case-sensitive-lowercase dispatch reality.; Verify SYS_DIRENTRY's start-LBA fields against the BIOS FNEXT post-state; source from the parsed entry if needed.; Factor the repeated LBA-setup / 16-bit inc-dec / slot-stride idioms into helpers to reclaim space under the 16K OS cap.; Emit a diagnostic when FSCK/PACK hit the 8-frame depth limit so a partial walk isn't reported as clean.


## Apps (assembler / compiler / editor)

### `apps/p8xasm.asm`  
**Code: B  ·  Docs: A**  ·  ASM

A standalone two-pass native assembler for the P8X that streams source in a line at a time and output a byte at a time through the BIOS file streams, so neither is bounded by RAM; it supports labels, equates, all operand shapes, the LDPn pseudo, directives (.org/.byte/.word/.ascii(z)/.fill), and both ;#use and .include appends.

This is genuinely strong, carefully-written hand assembler. The hard parts — 16-bit add/subtract/decrement with manual carry chaining, the two-pass model, the streamed EMIT with forward-.org zero-padding and backward-.org detection, and the delicate P2 save/restore around include splicing — are all correct on the paths I traced, and the carry/borrow branch senses (JC/JNC after CMP/SUB) are right everywhere I checked. The main dents are a real off-by-a-page boundary bug in the symbol-table-full check, a meaningful amount of dead code/vestigial machinery in a binary the project notes is now size-constrained, and a pattern of single-character dispatch that silently accepts malformed directives. Documentation is exceptional for hand ASM, though it under-labels the dead routines.

**Findings:**

- _medium_ · correctness · SYMDEFVAL, lines 702-705 — The table-full guard only compares the HIGH byte of SYMP against >SYMEND ($C0): 'LDA SYMP+1; LDB #>SYMEND; CMP; JC SD_FULL'. Any entry whose start is anywhere in page $BF passes the check, but a 14-byte entry starting as high as $BFFE writes through $C00B — 12 bytes past SYMEND ($C000) into the variable block (SRCPATH etc.). I confirmed the 1098th symbol (index 1097) starts at $BFFE and overflows by 12 bytes. So the real ceiling is one entry below the advertised ~1097, and hitting it corrupts variables instead of raising ?too many symbols. → Compare the full 16-bit SYMP against SYMEND-14 before appending (e.g. compute SYMP+14 and check it is <= SYMEND with a two-byte compare), rather than testing only the high byte. Given the project's noted symbol-table capacity pressure, a clean error at the true boundary matters.
- _low_ · efficiency · EMIT/EM_HW (1056-1068), INITPC (216-217), HIWAT decl (74) — The entire HIWAT max-tracking machinery is dead: HIWAT is reset in INITPC and updated on every EMIT, but nothing ever reads it — output length is driven exclusively by OUTPOS. This is per-byte wasted work (a full 16-bit max on every emitted byte) plus code bytes, in a binary the project memory notes is now bumping the code-SIZE ceiling. → Delete the EM_HW..EM_SET block, the HIWAT stores in INITPC, and the HIWAT variable, or document it as intentionally-retained vestigial state. Removing it shortens the hottest routine in pass 2.
- _low_ · clarity · WORDCOPY (1866), SKIPTOEOL (1603), EATNL (1617), SRCP (71) — Dead code/data not marked as such: WORDCOPY, SKIPTOEOL and EATNL have no callers (verified by grep — only self-references/comments), and SRCP is declared but never referenced. Their normal-looking headers invite a maintainer to assume they're part of the live design. In a size-constrained binary this is also wasted space. → Remove them, or if kept for reference, tag each header 'unused' the way SETFNOUT/OUTNAME already are.
- _low_ · correctness · DO_DIR (378-395), DD_ORG/DD_BYTE/DD_WORD/DD_ASC dispatch — Directives are dispatched on a single character (MNBUF+1): 'O'->.org, 'B'->.byte, 'W'->.word, 'A'->.ascii, 'F'->.fill. Any unsupported directive sharing that second letter is silently accepted and mis-assembled rather than reported — e.g. '.align' would dispatch to DD_ASC (via 'A'), '.org'/'.other' both hit 'O'. .ascii vs .asciiz is likewise distinguished only by MNBUF+6=='Z'. This works for the exact supported set but is fragile and gives no diagnostic on typos. → This is acceptable for a minimal on-target tool, but consider validating the full directive name (or at least the trailing NUL position) before dispatch so an unknown '.xxx' raises ?syntax instead of silently emitting wrong bytes. Low priority; note it as a known limitation if left as-is.
- _low_ · correctness · DOINSTR LDPn guard, lines 280-301 — The LDPn-pseudo detector checks MNBUF[0..2]=='L','D','P' and MNBUF+3 in '1'..'3' but never checks MNBUF+4==0, so a token like 'LDP1X' is routed to DO_LDP. Unverified as a real-world trigger since no such mnemonic exists and READTOK-upcased identifiers of that form are implausible; flagged for completeness. → Add a 'LDA MNBUF+4; JNZ DI_NORM' after the digit-range test to require the pseudo to be exactly 4 chars. Cheap and removes the ambiguity.
- _low_ · safety · ABSPATH (1778-1837), ARGTMP=$C070 (48), SRCPATH=$C000/OUTPATH=$C030 — ABSPATH concatenates SYS_GETCWD + '/' + ARGTMP into the 48-byte SRCPATH/OUTPATH slots with no length bound. A long CWD plus a relative arg can overrun SRCPATH ($C000) into OUTPATH ($C030) and beyond. Unverified in practice (depends on runtime CWD length), and typical for this class of tool, but there is no guard. There is also a theoretical DEP1 underflow at AB_TSL if SYS_GETCWD ever returns an empty string. → If path lengths could realistically approach ~46 chars, bound the copy against the 48-byte slot (and guard the empty-CWD DEP1). Otherwise document the fixed 48-byte path limit alongside the other limits in the header.

**Docs:** Outstanding for hand-written ASM: a thorough file header (invocation, syntax, memory-layout rationale, conventions, limits), a header comment on essentially every routine, and — most valuably — inline annotations that spell out the carry sense of each branch ("C=1 when OFFH >= OUTPOS+1", "SYMP hi >= SYMEND hi", "< '1'"), which is exactly what a reviewer needs on an ISA with no ADC/SBC. Variable and buffer maps at the top are precise and match the code. Gaps: (1) several routines are dead but not marked so — WORDCOPY, SKIPTOEOL, EATNL have normal-looking headers with no "unused" note (only SETFNOUT/OUTNAME are honestly flagged), so a maintainer can't tell they're unreachable; (2) the HIWAT machinery is documented as if live ("track HIWAT") but nothing consumes it; (3) the file header's claim that equates are fully "order-independent (two-pass)" overstates it — a forward equate reference resolves for label addresses but a *use* of an equate earlier in pass 2 than its defining line still sees the stale pass-1 value.

**Strengths:** Manual 16-bit arithmetic is uniformly correct: EVAL add/sub (773-808), the DF_GO fill decrement (457-466), EMITB's OUTPOS bump, and MUL10 (x2 + x8) all chain carry/borrow the right way, and I found no inverted JC/JNC after CMP/SUB anywhere.; The EMIT pad/emit logic (1026-1046) is a nicely-reasoned two-byte comparison that correctly emits when OUTPOS==off, zero-pads forward gaps, and traps backward .org — the tricky equal-high-byte fallthrough is handled correctly.; Pointer discipline is careful and consistent: OPCFIND/SYMFIND/SYMDEFVAL/EMIT all save and restore the P1 line cursor (SAVP/SAVP2/SAVPE), and SRCGET's save/restore of P2 around NEXTUSE — testing carry before the restore can disturb it — is exactly right.; The streamed two-pass architecture (source in via FOPEN/FGETB, output out via FPUTB, symbol table parked in the $8400 gap) is well thought out, and the ordering hazards around shared SBUF (FSDIRBUF repoint, FDELETE-before-FWOPEN, FRESOLVE-before-open in OUTINIT) are anticipated and documented.; OPCFIND's match logic correctly rejects both prefix directions (MNBUF a prefix of a table name, and a table name a prefix of MNBUF) via the dual end-of-string check at OF_NMEND.

**Top improvements:** Fix the symbol-table-full check in SYMDEFVAL to compare the full 16-bit SYMP (or SYMP+14) against SYMEND, closing the 12-byte overflow into the variable block at the top of the table.; Remove the dead HIWAT tracking from EMIT/INITPC and the unused routines/vars (WORDCOPY, SKIPTOEOL, EATNL, SRCP) to reclaim code space in a binary that is now near the size ceiling — or clearly mark them unused.; Tighten directive and LDPn dispatch (validate the full token / trailing NUL) so malformed '.xxx' or 'LDPn'-lookalikes raise ?syntax instead of being silently mis-assembled.; Correct the header's 'equates are order-independent' wording to reflect that a forward equate value is only guaranteed once its defining line has run in pass 2.


### `apps/p8xcc.asm`  
**Code: C  ·  Docs: B**  ·  ASM

A single-pass C compiler written in P8X assembly, dense enough to self-host on the machine. Streams C source through the BIOS read stream and emits P8X assembly to stdout, supporting a growing subset (ints, chars, pointers, arrays, structs, functions with an arg-stack ABI, control flow, //#use splicing and //#define macros).

An impressive, genuinely functional bootstrap compiler with consistent conventions and thorough header documentation. 16-bit math chains, signed slot arithmetic, and short-circuit/label threading are almost all carry-correct. But there is one clear control-flow fall-through bug in local variable declarations, the relational operators do unsigned comparison on signed ints, and there is dead/stale code and a few doc/code mismatches.

**Findings:**

- _high_ · correctness · st_decl, lines ~2895-2906 (sd_chkeq -> sd_arr) — A local scalar declaration with an initializer (int x = 5; / char c = 'a';) falls through into the array-declaration code. After the '=' branch does ADVANCE/GEXPR/EMITSTV (token now ';'), there is no jump past sd_arr, so control drops into sd_arr: JSR ADVANCE which consumes the ';', treats the next token's CURV as an array size (corrupting NLSLOT), marks the var as an array, and ADVANCEs again — eating the next statement's first token. Miscompiles/derails parsing for any local initialized declaration. → Insert JMP sd_semi after EMITSTV (line 2905) so the initialized-scalar path terminates instead of falling into sd_arr.
- _medium_ · correctness · MCMP_DEF (__cmp) + EMITCMP (ec_lt/ec_ge/ec_gt/ec_le) — Relational operators compile to __cmp then JC/JNC — UNSIGNED 16-bit comparison — but C int is signed. if (x < 0), a < b with negatives give wrong results. The ISA has signed BLT/BGE/BLE/BGT, unused. A real semantic gap. → Emit signed BLT/BGE/BLE/BGT for relational codegen, or document prominently that comparisons are unsigned.
- _low_ · clarity · EM_STSLOT (2552) + ARGSLOT (5671) — EM_STSLOT has no caller and ARGSLOT is read but never written. If called it would also be wrong: emits __V+ offset without the *2 scaling every other slot emit uses. Dead and latent-buggy. → Remove EM_STSLOT and ARGSLOT, or fix the missing 2x scaling and add the writer.
- _low_ · clarity · EM_VADDR (602), CHKSELFREC/SELFREC (4086/5584), MVL/MVF/MVWORD (4815-4817) — Multiple dead routines/data (unused/legacy) costing TPA space in a size-constrained self-hosting binary. → Delete the dead routines and strings.
- _low_ · efficiency · MMULDEF (__mul) and MDMDEF (__divmod) — Multiply is O(multiplier) repeated addition; divide/modulo is O(quotient) repeated subtraction. x*1000 or n/2 on large values costs thousands of iterations. → Consider shift-and-add multiply / restoring-shift division, or note the cost in the header.
- _low_ · correctness · //#use / //#define splice + lexer pushback (PBF/PBC, SAVESTATE/RESTORESTATE) — One-char pushback state isn't part of the saved/restored read-stream state across a splice/EOF pop; a pending pushback at a boundary could leak across streams. Likely unreachable in practice — unverified, low severity. → Include PBF/PBC in SAVESTATE/RESTORESTATE, or assert PBF==0 at splice/pop.

**Docs:** File header and per-routine headers are excellent (ABI, slot model, re-entrancy, carry conventions). Deductions: several comments name helpers that no longer exist (GTERM says via __mul8/__divmod8; gt_div/gt_mod say __d1/__d0 but helpers are __div/__mod returning __ax; EM_SAVESLOTS/EM_RESTSLOTS headers say SYMCNT but the code loops over NLSLOT; MMULDEF described as 8-bit multiply but is 16-bit repeated-add). EM_VADDR self-labeled 'unused'; MVL/MVF/MVWORD labeled 'legacy'.

**Strengths:** Carry/borrow discipline correct in the hard places: 16-bit EN_DIG subtract, __add/__sub/__cmp, EM_INCVAR/EM_DECVAR, and the signed 'global slot - SLOTBASE' math all get JC/JNC-after-SUB right.; Clever balanced use of the system stack (P3 via PHA/PLA) to hold label numbers across recursive descent in if/else/for/while/ternary/&&/|| — stack-balanced on every path.; Strong architecture for a tiny target: single __V storage array, static per-function slots + runtime arg-stack ABI (forward calls, mutual recursion), caller-saved slots skipped when an address is taken, bounded MAXFUNC guard with clean bailout.; Exceptionally documented for hand assembly.

**Top improvements:** Fix the local initialized-declaration fall-through (add JMP sd_semi) — a real miscompile of common C.; Use signed BLT/BGE/BLE/BGT for relational codegen, or clearly document comparisons are unsigned.; Remove dead code (EM_VADDR, EM_STSLOT/ARGSLOT, CHKSELFREC/SELFREC, MVL/MVF/MVWORD) and reconcile stale comments.


### `apps/p8xedit.asm`  
**Code: B  ·  Docs: A**  ·  P8X ASSEMBLY (8-BIT TTL ISA)

A standalone line-oriented text editor for the P8X transient program area: loads/edits/writes a root filesystem file through the BIOS jump table, operating on a flat LF-separated text buffer with L/A/I/D/W/Q/? commands.

This is careful, well-structured assembly. Multi-byte arithmetic (which must chain carry/borrow by hand on this ISA) is done correctly throughout, carry/borrow branch senses are right in every spot I checked, and the overlap-safe backward copy in GAPOPEN is genuinely well done. The real weaknesses are missing bounds checks at the two untrusted-size boundaries — console line input and file load — either of which can overrun a fixed buffer into adjacent state. Correctness of the core logic is high; the gaps are in defensive input validation, plus a documented-but-unenforced 255-line ceiling.

**Findings:**

- _medium_ · safety · GETLINE (lines 393-433) — No bound on line length. LBUF is one page ($BE00-$BEFF) and the variable block begins immediately at $BF00 (TENDL). Storing (P2)+ has no cap, so a line of 256+ characters walks P2 into $BF00 and the CR handler's STA (P2) (line 432) then writes the null terminator over TENDL/editor state. A pasted or long line silently corrupts the buffer-end pointer. → Guard the store: compare P2 low byte against a max (e.g. #$FE) before STA (P2)+ and ignore/beep on further input, reserving room for the terminator. Cap at 254 to stay clear of the STRLEN+1 wrap noted below.
- _medium_ · correctness · LOADFILE (lines 338-351) — FLOADAT reads FLEN bytes into TBUF with no check that FLEN <= the 12KB ceiling ($C000..$F000). A file larger than 12KB overruns TBUF past $F000 into whatever lives above, and TEND is then set beyond the ceiling, so subsequent ROOMQ/CHKINS math operates on an already-invalid buffer. → Before FLOADAT, compare FLEN (16-bit, plus FLEN+2 for the 24-bit case) against $3000 and refuse/truncate oversized files with an error message rather than loading them.
- _low_ · correctness · CI_INS / STRLEN + ADD #1 (lines 277-279) — K = line length + 1. If a 255-char line reaches here, ADD #1 wraps K to 0; GAPOPEN then opens a 0-byte gap while the write loop still stores 255 chars + LF, corrupting following text. Only reachable if GETLINE's missing length cap (above) is fixed to allow 255. Unverified as currently reachable given the buffer geometry, but the arithmetic is fragile. → Enforce a max line length (<=254) in GETLINE so K can never reach 256, or range-check K before GAPOPEN.
- _low_ · correctness · Line numbering / LINENO (lines 136-137, 172-174) and ARGN (8-bit) — The 12KB buffer can hold well over 255 short lines, but LINENO is 8-bit (INC wraps 255->0 during L) and ARGN is 8-bit, so D/I cannot address lines past 255. The header states 'max 255 lines' but nothing prevents A from creating a 256th line, after which listing shows a wrapped/duplicate number and those lines are unaddressable. → Either enforce the 255-line cap in CMD_A (reject appends once 255 lines exist) or accept it explicitly; at minimum note the failure mode near CMD_A.
- _low_ · isa-misuse · CONOUT/CONIN/PUTS pointer preservation (CMD_L CL_PR loop ~line 162; GETLINE ~lines 408-409) — The listing loop keeps the line cursor in P1 across JSR CONOUT, and GETLINE keeps the write cursor in P2 across CONIN/CONOUT. This is correct ONLY if those BIOS routines preserve P1/P2. That contract is assumed but not stated in the BIOS entry-point list at the top. Unverified here — if it holds (as the consistent usage implies) there is no bug; flagging so the assumption is documented. → Add a one-line note to the BIOS entry table that CONIN/CONOUT/PUTS preserve P1/P2 (or, if uncertain, save/restore the cursor around the calls).
- _low_ · clarity · CMD_D / CMD_I with ARGN==0 (LINESTART LS_Z0, line 619-625) — An argument of 0 (or a bare 'D'/'I' with no number, since PARSEARG defaults ARGN to 0) is silently treated as line 1. 'D' with no arg deletes line 1, which may surprise a user expecting an error. → Optionally treat ARGN==0 as 'no such line' for D, or document the 0->1 coercion in the header command list.

**Docs:** Excellent for hand assembly. Strong file header explaining entry ABI, command set, and buffer geometry; every routine has a header describing its contract (inputs, outputs, side effects like "TMP2 = found?"). Inline comments accurately state the non-obvious flag intent ("C = c >= '0'", "borrow flag: 1 if the lo-byte SUB borrowed") and the DECOUT digit-suppression comment (lines 519-521) correctly explains the TMP2 repurposing. Minor gaps: the 255-char practical line limit and the >12KB-file overflow risk are not documented anywhere despite being real constraints; the "page-aligned for cheap at start?" comment (line 48) oversells the optimization since the code still compares both bytes.

**Strengths:** Every multi-byte add/subtract chains carry/borrow by hand correctly (LOADFILE, CHKINS, GAPOPEN, CMD_W) with the right JC/JNC sense — no inverted-branch bugs found, which is the most common failure class on this ISA.; GAPOPEN does a proper top-down, pre-decrement backward copy so the overlapping source/destination ranges never clobber unmoved bytes, and the loop's 16-bit CNT decrement/termination (OR of both halves) is correct.; Consistent Z-flag return convention (LDA #0 for true / LDA #1 for false in PTR_AT_END, ROOMQ, CHKINS, LINESTART) makes callers uniform and readable.; Append/insert correctly stash the edit cursor in SRC across GETLINE calls that clobber P2, with a clear comment explaining exactly why (lines 179-180).; Strong, accurate documentation: file header, per-routine contracts, and flag-intent comments that match the code.

**Top improvements:** Add a length cap to GETLINE so a long console line cannot overrun LBUF into the variable page at $BF00.; Validate FLEN against the 12KB ceiling in LOADFILE before FLOADAT, rejecting oversized files instead of overrunning TBUF.; Enforce (or explicitly bound) the 255-line / 254-char limits so ARGN, LINENO, and the STRLEN+1 gap size can never wrap.; Document the assumed P1/P2 preservation contract of CONIN/CONOUT/PUTS in the BIOS entry-point list.


## BASIC

### `basic/p8xbasic.asm`  
**Code: B  ·  Docs: A**  ·  ASM (P8X 8-BIT TTL ASSEMBLY)

A self-contained Tiny-BASIC interpreter for the P8X TTL computer: tokenizing line editor, sorted line storage with rebuild-to-scratch editing, recursive-descent 16-bit integer expression evaluator, string variables/functions, FOR/GOSUB, and optional BIOS-backed SAVE/LOAD and sequential data files.

This is a well-organized, carefully written interpreter. The 16-bit arithmetic (add/sub/mul/div, two's-complement negate, signed comparison via $80-bias) is correct, multi-byte carry/borrow is chained properly, and I found NO inverted JC/JNC branches after CMP/SUB — a class of bug that is easy to introduce on this ISA and was clearly avoided with discipline. Pointer save/restore around routines that walk tables with P2/P1 is consistent and correct. The main weaknesses are functional rather than low-level: FOR/NEXT does not handle negative STEP, and there are no overflow guards on the fixed GOSUB/FOR/variable tables (silent corruption past the hard limits). The code also fights the ISA in one pervasive way — it reloads (P2) before every CMP in its long if-else dispatchers even though CMP preserves A — which wastes significant code space and cycles.

**Findings:**

- _medium_ · correctness · DONEXT nx_loop/nx_done, lines 1196-1211 — NEXT computes var=var+step then always terminates when var>limit (signed), regardless of the sign of STEP. A countdown loop such as FOR I=10 TO 1 STEP -1 terminates after the first iteration (9 > 1 -> nx_done), so negative STEP is broken even though STEP is an advertised feature (HELP text, line 4118). → Branch on the sign of FSTEP: for step>=0 finish when var>limit; for step<0 finish when var<limit (i.e. when limit>var). You already have the biased signed compare; just select the terminating condition based on FSTEP+1 bit7.
- _medium_ · safety · DOGOSUB ~950, DOFOR fp_alloc ~1077, VARFIND vf_new ~1878, SVARFIND svf2new ~2644 — None of the fixed tables are bounds-checked before appending. A 4th GOSUB (GSTK holds 3), 3rd FOR (FSTK holds 2), 33rd variable (NVARS=32), or 17th string var (NSVARS=16) writes past the table into adjacent scratch/state — silent memory corruption rather than a diagnostic. E.g. GSTK+GSP*4 with GSP=3 lands on GSP itself; VARTAB+i*8 with i=32 overflows the low byte back to VARTAB[0]. → Before each append, compare the depth/count against the capacity and jump to an error (e.g. ?OUT OF MEMORY / ?TOO MANY, and set ENDF when running). Even a single guard per table would convert corruption into a clean error.
- _medium_ · efficiency · STMT 244-338; also FACTOR 1511+, SPEEK 2503+, STERM 2912+, EVAL 1248+, CKLEAD 3742+ — Each dispatcher reloads the test byte with LDA (P2) before every LDB #TOK / CMP / JZ triple. Per the ISA, CMP preserves A and LDB writes only B, so A already holds the char across the whole chain — every reload after the first is dead work. STMT alone does ~20 redundant memory reads per statement dispatch; the pattern repeats in six routines and inflates code size noticeably. → Load the byte once (LDA (P2)) then emit only LDB #tok / CMP / JZ pairs. Better still, replace the long comparison chains with a small (token,handler-addr) dispatch table walked in a loop, which would shrink the statement/factor dispatch dramatically.
- _low_ · correctness · STMT implicit-LET gate, lines 331-338 — The bare-variable fallthrough tests the raw byte against 'A'..'Z' with no UPCHAR, unlike VARGET/SPEEK/CKLEAD/DONEXT which upcase first. Combined with MATCHKW being case-sensitive (KWTAB is uppercase and input is never folded), the interpreter is effectively uppercase-only, and a lowercase implicit assignment like 'a=5' is rejected with ?WHAT. This is an inconsistency more than a hazard, but it is undocumented. → Either fold input to uppercase once in GETLINE/CRUNCH (outside string literals) so the whole language is case-insensitive, or state in the header that only uppercase is accepted and make this gate consistent.
- _low_ · correctness · DOINPUT, lines 804-828 (save/restore P2 via GTMP around GETLINE) — GETLINE reuses LBUF as its buffer. During RUN this is safe because P2 points into PROG, but for an INPUT statement typed at the immediate prompt, P2 (saved in GTMP) points into LBUF, which GETLINE then overwrites with the reply. After restore, STMTLINE resumes parsing from a clobbered buffer, so 'INPUT X : PRINT X' entered directly would misbehave. Unverified as to whether immediate INPUT is a supported use case. → If immediate-mode INPUT is meant to work, read the reply into a separate small buffer rather than LBUF, or note that INPUT is only supported inside a RUN.
- _low_ · clarity · DORUN, lines 622-633 — RUN resets ENDF/GSP/FSP/FMODE/OUTFILE but not VARCNT/SVARCNT, so variables retain their values from a previous run. Auto-creation zeroes a var only on first-ever reference, so a second RUN sees stale values — surprising versus common BASIC semantics that clear variables on RUN. → Decide and document the intended semantics; if fresh state is wanted, also zero VARCNT and SVARCNT in DORUN (mirroring NEWPROG).

**Docs:** Excellent documentation overall. Strong file header (build targets, PROG record format, edit strategy), a complete annotated memory map with byte offsets, and a clear per-routine comment on essentially every routine including register/pointer contracts (e.g. "Uses P2 as the walker (FGETB clobbers P1, not P2)"). Inline comments are accurate where I spot-checked them, including the tricky signed-bias compare ("bias by $8000 -> signed ordering") and the historical note explaining the JUMPADDR/PCNT aliasing bug. Gaps: the NEXT comments (lines 1208-1211) describe only the positive-STEP case and never mention that negative STEP is unsupported; the fact that NEXT ignores its variable argument is noted only obliquely ("result ignored"); nothing documents the hard table capacities (3 GOSUB / 2 FOR / 32 vars / 16 string vars) as un-checked limits; and the effectively uppercase-only nature of keywords/leaders is not stated.

**Strengths:** 16-bit arithmetic is correct: two's-complement negate (fa_neg/ABS/VAL), byte-by-byte carry/borrow chaining in ADD16/SUB16/MUL16/DIV16, and signed comparison via $80 high-byte bias are all implemented right.; No inverted carry/borrow branches: every JC/JNC after CMP/SUB I checked (REPL digit test, FACTOR, HEXDIG, UPCHAR, ISALNUM, SUBSTR/SAPP/FREADREC length caps, DIV16) matches the firmware's C-semantics.; Disciplined pointer hygiene: routines that need P1/P2 to walk tables consistently save and restore the parse cursor on the CPU stack (VARFIND, SVARFIND, SMOVE, SAPP, SUBSTR, SETFNAME, EVALSTR), and PUTCH saves/restores P1 around FPUTB.; Keyword tokenizer is robust: MATCHKW guards against matching a prefix of a longer identifier (ISALNUM check after the keyword) and the '$'-suffixed string keywords are encoded cleanly in KWTAB.; Documentation is genuinely strong — full memory map, per-routine contracts, and honest historical bug notes (the PCNT/JUMPADDR aliasing comment).

**Top improvements:** Fix FOR/NEXT to honor the sign of STEP so descending loops work (select the terminating comparison on FSTEP's sign).; Add capacity guards to the GOSUB, FOR, variable, and string-variable tables so overflow raises a clean error instead of silently corrupting adjacent state.; Stop reloading (P2) before every CMP in the dispatchers (CMP preserves A); ideally convert the long token comparison chains to a table-driven dispatch to reclaim code space.; Resolve the case-sensitivity story: fold input to uppercase once (outside string literals) for a case-insensitive language, or document the uppercase-only requirement and make the STMT implicit-LET gate consistent with the other name paths.


## Command twins — hand ASM

### `os/commands-asm/awk.asm`  
**Code: B  ·  Docs: B**  ·  P8X ASSEMBLY

Hand-coded /BIN awk command: parses `awk [-F c] '[/re/]{print items}' [file]`, reads records from a file or stdin, splits into fields, optionally matches a regex, and interprets a minimal print action ($0 $N $NF NF NR "str").

Solid, careful hand assembly. The parts most likely to harbor bugs on this ISA — the manual multi-byte carry/borrow chains (16-bit nr increment, the 16-bit compare-and-subtract in pd_dig, and every base+index address computation) — are all correct, with no inverted JC/JNC branches. The main weaknesses are hygiene rather than correctness: a fully dead address-computation block in pfield, an entirely dead `pp` variable, one counter (`pl`) that is never explicitly initialized and works only because .fill emits zeros into the reloaded image, and a fair amount of redundant reloading that the ISA's "CMP preserves A" guarantee makes unnecessary. Documentation is above average for hand asm.

**Findings:**

- _medium_ · efficiency · pfield, lines 621-627 — Dead code: the first address computation (LDA pf_st; LDB pf_j; ADD; LDB #<line; ADD; TAP1L; LDA #>line) is completely overwritten by the recomputation at lines 629-640 before P1 is ever dereferenced. It is also wrong on its own (no carry into the high byte), so it only looks correct because the block below replaces it. Executes once per character printed. → Delete lines 621-627 (through the second `LDA #>line`) and let the loop fall straight into the pf_ix computation at line 629.
- _medium_ · clarity · a_prog (lines 96-99) and a_pdone (line 146) — The 16-bit variable `pp` is written but never read. Lines 96-99 initialize pp to the prog[] pointer, but the copy loop uses (P2) as source and recomputes prog+pl inside prog_put, so pp is unused. Line 146 (`LDA #0; STA pp`) then clobbers it for no effect, and its comment claims pp is 'used as scratch below' which is untrue. → Remove the pp initialization (lines 96-99), the STA pp at line 146, and the `pp: .fill 2` declaration entirely.
- _low_ · correctness · pl (used in prog_put line 245; never STA'd before first use) — Unlike the other index counters (rei/acti zeroed in parse_prog; nr/nf/si/li/aci explicitly zeroed), `pl` is never initialized to 0 before the program-copy loop runs. It works only because `pl: .fill 1` emits a zero byte that is loaded fresh into TPA on every exec. This is fragile and inconsistent, and contradicts the 'uninitialized RAM' comment. Not exploitable today (each command run reloads the image) but a foot-gun if the routine is ever re-entered without reload. → Add an explicit `LDA #0 / STA pl` near a_prog (line 96), matching how the other counters are initialized, and correct the scratch-section comment.
- _low_ · efficiency · a_pu / a_pql program-copy loops (lines 112-140) — P2 and the aarg cursor are advanced in lockstep (INP2 plus JSR aarg_inc on every byte). Since P2 is reloaded from aarg at the start of every subsequent stage anyway, maintaining P2 via INP2 here is redundant work. → Advance only aarg in the copy loops and reload P2 from aarg once per byte if needed, or keep only P2 and derive aarg at the end; either removes one increment per character.
- _low_ · efficiency · multiple: readrec 404, ra_str 817, a_Fc 80, and similar — Several `LDA (Pn)` / `JSR *_ch` reloads immediately follow a CMP that, per the ISA, preserves A. The value is still in A, so the reload is wasted (e.g. line 817 re-fetches the string char that A already holds after the CMP at 814-815; line 80 re-reads (P2) that A already holds after line 78). → Where a store into a named scratch var isn't also needed, rely on CMP preserving A and drop the redundant reload. Low priority; the current form is defensively clear.
- _low_ · efficiency · prog_ch/act_ch/act_at/line_ch and ~8 inlined copies — The base+index -> P1 pointer computation (LDA #<base; LDB idx; ADD; TAP1L; LDA #>base; JNC; INC; TAP1H) is duplicated ~12 times across parse_prog, emit_field, pfield, etc., bloating the image. → Consider a single helper that takes base (in a zero-page pair) + an index byte and returns P1, or at least reuse the existing *_ch helpers where the base matches. Judgment call given call overhead on this CPU.
- _low_ · correctness · run_action ra_it (lines 795-796) vs unknown-item path (line 807) — rfirst is cleared to 0 at ra_it before the item type is known. For an unrecognized item character the code falls to line 807 (skip one char) having already cleared rfirst even though nothing was printed. This can cause a spurious leading OFS space before the next real item, or suppress the empty-`print` $0 fallback, on malformed input. → Move the `STA rfirst` (clear) to after each branch that actually emits output, or restore rfirst on the unknown-item path. Minor, only affects malformed actions.
- _low_ · clarity · ra_pr, lines 758-766 — The 'print' keyword is detected by first character only ('p') and then blindly skips 5 bytes. An action like `put $1` would be misparsed (skips 'put $'). Matches the minimal awk intent but is silently non-robust. → Optionally verify the full 'print' token, or add a comment stating that only the leading 'p' is checked by design.
- _low_ · isa-misuse · ra_fdl *10 build, lines 852-862 — Unverified: the *10 build uses three consecutive SHL on fnum to form *8. The firmware header says 'SHL/SHR/ROL/ROR shift through carry.' If SHL shifts the previous carry INTO bit0 (rather than shifting in 0), leftover carry from the earlier `SHL; STA ftmp` would corrupt the *8 term. Because ROL/ROR exist separately as rotate-through-carry, SHL almost certainly shifts in 0 and this is fine — flagging only because the header wording is ambiguous. → Confirm SHL semantics against docs/p8x-system-design.md; if SHL rotates carry in, insert CLC before the shift sequence. Very likely a non-issue.
