**Docs:** Strong file header (usage, parse order, state layout) and good per-routine headers; pnum/pd_dig and readrec/split are especially well explained, and the carry/borrow reasoning is documented inline. Deductions: (1) line 628's comment ("recompute high with carry") describes and half-excuses a block of dead code rather than flagging it; (2) the closing scratch comment "uninitialized RAM" is inaccurate — .fill emits zero bytes that ARE loaded, and the code in fact depends on that for `pl`; (3) line 146's "pp used as scratch below — re-point" is misleading, since pp is never read again. Non-obvious parts are otherwise well covered.

**Strengths:** All manual multi-byte carry/borrow chains are correct — 16-bit nr increment (185-192), aarg increment, and especially the 16-bit compare+subtract in pd_dig (689-716) — with no inverted JC/JNC branches, which is the dominant bug class on this ISA.; Every array access is bounds-capped before the store (prog@127, re@79, act@119, line@255, fstart/flen@40), and the NUL terminators land exactly within the allocated .fill sizes.; pfield's field-index guard (fi==nf via JZ, fi>nf via JC) correctly distinguishes the equal and greater cases, and $0 vs $N vs $NF are handled cleanly.; Good documentation discipline for hand assembly: a real file header describing parse order and state, plus per-routine headers and inline reasoning about carry semantics.; Sensible register economy — P2 as the tail cursor, P1 as the scratch pointer inside every helper, with helpers consistently avoiding P2 clobbers so callers stay valid across JSR.

**Top improvements:** Delete the dead address-computation block in pfield (lines 621-627); it runs per character, is internally buggy, and is fully overwritten by the block below it.; Remove the dead `pp` variable and its writes (lines 96-99, 146, and the .fill), and fix the misleading 'used as scratch' comment.; Explicitly initialize `pl` to 0 before the program copy, matching the other counters, and correct the 'uninitialized RAM' scratch comment to reflect that .fill zeros are loaded and depended upon.; Trim redundant post-CMP reloads and the lockstep P2/aarg advancement to reclaim cycles and bytes.


### `os/commands-asm/cat.asm`  
**Code: A  ·  Docs: A**  ·  P8X ASSEMBLY

Hand-coded CAT command: prints one named file, glob-expands and prints each match, or filters stdin to stdout when given no argument. ASM counterpart of os/commands/cat.c.

This is a clean, correct, well-documented piece of hand assembly. Every carry/borrow branch is used correctly (a notable achievement given the ISA has no add-with-carry and this class of code is bug-prone), pointer clobbers across subroutine calls are handled by keeping all loop state in memory variables, and the control flow terminates cleanly on every path with no stack imbalance. I found no correctness bugs. The only issues are minor behavioral/efficiency nuances: over-broad help-flag matching, a slightly wasteful space-skip loop, and no conventional "-"-means-stdin handling. Code and docs are both strong.

**Findings:**

- _low_ · clarity · c_chk (lines 36-47) — The help detection only compares the single character immediately after '-' against 'h'/'H'. Any word beginning "-h" or "-H" (e.g. "-help", "-hazard", "-h.txt") prints usage, and there is no end-of-word check. This is a behavioral surprise, not a crash. Whether it matches os/commands/cat.c is unverified. → If exact "-h"/"-H" is intended, after matching the flag char, advance P2 once more and confirm the next byte is a word terminator (NUL/CR/space) before jumping to c_usage; otherwise fall through to the filename path. If the loose match is intentional, note it in the c_chk comment.
- _low_ · clarity · c_noarg / c_single (lines 51-61, 129-138) — A bare argument of "-" is treated as a literal filename and yields "cat: not found", rather than the conventional Unix behavior where "-" means stdin. May be intentional and may match the C version (unverified), but it is a latent usability gap worth confirming. → Decide whether "-" should route to c_stdin; if so, special-case a lone '-' followed by a word terminator before the glob scan. If the divergence from convention is deliberate, a one-line comment would prevent future 'fixes'.
- _low_ · efficiency · c_sk (lines 17-34) — Each space-skip iteration reloads P2 from c_arg (4 instrs, lines 17-20) and re-derives the pointer, then does a full 16-bit increment of c_arg. Since P2 already points at the current char, the reload is redundant per-iteration work. The scan loop (c_scl) by contrast walks with INP2, which is cheaper. → Walk with INP2 during skipping (as c_scl does) and write c_arg back to P2 once when the first non-space is found — or keep c_arg in sync via INP2 plus a mirrored 16-bit inc — avoiding the per-iteration reload. Purely an optimization; current code is correct.
- _low_ · style · whole file — Repeated LDB #imm / CMP / Jcc comparison chains (c_scl compares against 0, 13, 32, '*', '?') are verbose but idiomatic for a single-B-register ISA; not a defect, just noting there is no cheaper form available. → No action required; the ISA forces this pattern. Mentioned only so it is not mistaken for accidental duplication.

**Docs:** Excellent for hand ASM. File header explains purpose, the ;#use dependencies, and the P2=arg-tail entry convention. Every routine/label block has an accurate lead comment, and the non-obvious bits are explained correctly: the 16-bit carry-propagating pointer advance (lines 25/30), the CMP flag semantics for the loop bound ("CMP sets C when A>=B, i.e. i>=ge_cnt", line 113), open_path/catpath return codes, and the FGETB/SYS_GETC EOF-in-carry convention. catpath has a proper In/Out/Clobbers header. I spot-checked the comments against the code and found no misleading ones. Only gap: the c_chk comment ("Handle the -h/-H help flag") does not mention that the match is only on the single char after '-', so "-help"/"-hxyz" also trigger usage — a reader could assume an exact match.

**Strengths:** All four carry/borrow branches (JNC after ADD, JC after CMP, JC after both EOF-in-carry syscalls) are used correctly — no inverted-carry bug in a code class where that is the common failure.; 16-bit pointer advance in c_sk correctly propagates carry from the low byte into the high byte (lines 25-33).; Pointer-clobber safety is handled cleanly: catpath documents that it clobbers P1/P2, and every caller keeps loop state (c_i, ge_cnt, op_a, gidx) in memory, re-deriving pointers after each call. The glob loop re-reads ge_cnt each iteration.; Control flow is disciplined: every exit path ends in RTS, no PHA/PLA imbalance, no fall-through into data.; Documentation is unusually thorough and accurate for hand assembly, including a proper In/Out/Clobbers contract for catpath and correct explanation of the CMP loop-bound semantics.; Magic numbers are mostly self-documenting (ASCII literals '-', 'h', '*' used directly; 32/13/10 annotated in comments as space/CR/LF).

**Top improvements:** Tighten (or explicitly document) the -h/-H help match so it does not silently accept any "-h..." word.; Confirm the intended semantics of a lone "-" argument (literal filename vs. stdin) and comment the decision.; Optionally streamline the c_sk space-skip loop to use INP2 instead of reloading P2 from c_arg each iteration.


### `os/commands-asm/cmp.asm`  
**Code: A  ·  Docs: A**  ·  P8X ASSEMBLY

Hand-coded /BIN CMP command: slurps file1 (<=8 KB) into a buffer, then streams file2 comparing byte-for-byte, reporting the first differing byte and its 1-based offset and line number (silent when the files are identical). ASM counterpart of os/commands/cmp.c.

This is a strong, careful piece of hand assembly. Every multi-byte carry/borrow branch I checked (the file-size cap, the off-vs-n1 length comparisons at both EOF paths, and the 16-bit subtract in the decimal printer) is correct — no inverted JC/JNC, which is the dominant bug class here. Pointer usage across FGETB/FRESOLVE/FOPEN calls is safe because state lives in memory vars and P1 is recomputed each iteration; the stack stays balanced (outcomes are reached by JMP and each RTSes exactly once to the OS). The only substantive gap is an unbounded copy into the 80-byte path buffer, and there are a couple of trivial redundancies. Documentation is excellent and accurate.

**Findings:**

- _medium_ · safety · abspath / ab_cp (lines 297-305, 405-466) — The copy into the 80-byte `path` buffer (SYS_GETCWD prefix + '/' + relative token) has no length bound. `path: .fill 80` sits immediately before `b1`, so a deep CWD plus a long filename can overflow past 80 bytes and corrupt the start of the file-1 buffer (or the SYS_GETCWD copy itself can overrun). → Cap total writes to path (e.g. track a counter against a PATHMAX and stop/error), or at minimum document the 80-byte limit and the fact that it must exceed the OS's max CWD+name. Confirm the C counterpart shares this limit; if so this is a project-wide convention worth flagging.
- _low_ · efficiency · line 159 — `LDA (P1)` reloads b1[off] that was already in A from line 155; since the spec guarantees CMP preserves A and JNZ does not touch A, the accumulator still holds b1[off] at this point. → Drop line 159 and go straight to `LDB #10 / CMP` to save a memory read per matched byte in the hot compare loop.
- _low_ · correctness · ab_sld (lines 431-432) — UNVERIFIED edge case: if SYS_GETCWD ever returns an empty string, ab_sld does DEP1 before the buffer start and reads a byte outside `ap_out`. In practice CWD is always at least "/", so this cannot trigger, but the routine has no guard. → No change needed if CWD is guaranteed non-empty; otherwise add a check that P1 != ap_out before DEP1. Marking low and unverified since it depends on SYS_GETCWD's contract.
- _low_ · clarity · c_chk (lines 31-41) — When arg starts with '-' but is not -h/-H, INP2 advances P2 and the code falls through to c_f1, treating '-x' (or a lone '-') as a filename. This works only because everything downstream reads from `carg`, not the advanced P2 — a fragile coupling that is easy to break in future edits. → Add a one-line comment noting that the INP2 advance is intentionally discarded (downstream uses carg), or restructure so P2 is not left dangling.

**Docs:** Clear file header stating purpose, the 8 KB cap, the streaming strategy, and the exact BIOS/OS entry points used. Nearly every helper (b1_ptr, the *_inc trio, set_ap, openf, pnum, pd_dig, abspath) has a concise routine header, and the inline comments explaining the carry logic (e.g. lines 81, 108-113, 147-153, 166-176) are accurate and match the code I verified — including the correct C>=B / no-borrow semantics. Minor gaps: no note that `path` is unbounded (80 bytes) and can overflow into b1 with a deep CWD; the redundant reload at line 159 is uncommented; the lone-'-' / '-x' fall-through behavior at c_chk isn't documented.

**Strengths:** All multi-byte carry/borrow branches are correct — the 8 KB cap check (105-113), both off-vs-n1 length comparisons (144-176), b1_ptr's carry propagation (249-263), and the 16-bit subtract-and-borrow in pd_dig (372-383) — with no inverted JC/JNC, the most common bug class for this ISA.; Stack discipline is clean: helpers are called with JSR/RTS, outcome handlers are entered by JMP and RTS exactly once to the OS, so there is no imbalance across the many branch targets.; Edge cases hold up: empty file1, both-empty (silent), file2-longer, and file1-longer all resolve to the correct message, and the store cap correctly writes indices 0..8191 without overflowing b1 while still counting n1 to detect >8 KB.; Documentation is genuinely good — accurate inline carry-logic comments and per-routine headers make a dense hand-asm program readable.; Correct, idiomatic ISA use: CMP-preserves-A is exploited, post-increment (Pn)+ used for buffer copies, and the pnum/pd_dig decimal printer is a clean repeated-subtract with proper leading-zero suppression and forced ones-digit.

**Top improvements:** Bound the path copy against an explicit PATHMAX (or document the invariant) so a deep CWD + long filename cannot overflow the 80-byte `path` buffer into b1.; Remove the redundant `LDA (P1)` at line 159 to shave a read from the inner compare loop.; Add a comment (or guard) documenting that the '-' fall-through at c_chk deliberately discards the P2 advance because downstream reads carg.


### `os/commands-asm/cp.asm`  
**Code: B  ·  Docs: B**  ·  P8X ASSEMBLY (8-BIT TTL ISA)

Hand-coded /BIN `cp` command: argument/flag parse, abspath resolution, single-file byte copy, glob-expansion copy-into-directory, and recursive directory-tree copy using depth-indexed per-level scratch arrays (P3 hardware stack holds only return addresses).

This is a careful, competent piece of hand-assembly. The hard parts — manual multi-byte carry chaining, and recursion that keeps per-level state in depth-indexed arrays so the tiny hardware stack only holds return addresses — are done correctly and with genuine discipline (parent ct_s/ct_d are saved into sp[d]/dp[d] before the global jsrc/jdst buffers are reused, and the shared $E000 dir buffer is safe to reuse across levels because each level fully buffers its listing in pass one before recursing). Every JC/JNC I checked is on the correct side of the carry. The weaknesses are all around bounds/robustness: recursion depth, per-directory entry count, and filename length are all capped by array sizing but not actually guarded, so pathological inputs corrupt memory or silently drop files rather than erroring. Documentation is above average but contains one materially misleading claim.

**Findings:**

- _medium_ · safety · copy_tree recursion, lines 666-668 (w_depth INC) — Recursion depth (w_depth) is incremented before each recursive JSR copy_tree with no upper-bound check. The per-depth arrays hold exactly 8 levels (sp/dp = 8*80, names = 8*288, isd = 8*24, nn/cidx = 8). A directory tree deeper than 8 levels drives w_depth to 8+, so spd_a computes sp+8*80 = the start of dp, names_a/isd_a overflow into neighbouring arrays, and the copy silently corrupts adjacent scratch/BSS. → Guard the recursion: before INC w_depth, compare w_depth to 7 (max index) and if at the limit, skip the recurse (and ideally print a 'too deep' diagnostic) instead of descending.
- _medium_ · correctness · ct_tl / ct_padl, lines 557-591 — Directory-entry names are copied into fixed 12-byte slots. The copy stops only on a space (32) or when ctk reaches 12; NUL-termination happens only in the ct_padl (space-found) path. A dirent name that fills all 12 bytes with no trailing space (the type byte is at de+12, so the name field is de[0..11] = 12 bytes) leaves the slot unterminated, so joinp's name-copy loop (which reads until NUL) runs past the slot into the next entry, producing a wrong destination path. UNVERIFIED: depends on the on-disk dirent format — whether names can actually reach 12 chars and whether they are always space-padded. If the FS guarantees <=11-char names or a pad byte at de[11], this cannot trigger. → Confirm the max dirent name length. If 12 is reachable, either widen the slot stride to 13 and always write a terminator, or clamp the copy to 11 chars + forced NUL. Cross-check byte-for-byte against os/commands/cp.c.
- _low_ · safety · ct_nl entry loop, lines 549-551 — When a directory has more than 24 entries, entries past the 24th are silently dropped (the count>=24 guard skips buffering but keeps iterating), so `cp -r` on a large directory copies an incomplete tree with no error. This is a real data-completeness limitation, not just a doc nit. → At minimum document the 24-entry cap in the header and/or emit a warning when the cap is hit; ideally raise the per-level capacity or loop the listing in batches.
- _low_ · correctness · copy_file, lines 433-470; callers at 314 and 682 — copy_file only reports failure for the source open (returns A=1 on FOPEN carry). A failed dst FWOPEN, and any FPUTB write error, are not detected. Additionally the copy_file return value is ignored entirely in the glob loop (314) and the tree walk (682), so mid-tree copy failures are swallowed and `cp` still exits success. → Return a distinct status for dst-open/write failure and have the tree/glob loops surface it (accumulate an error flag and print once at the end).
- _low_ · correctness · cf_done, lines 465-466 — Only a single FCLOSE (A=0) is issued, but copy_file has both a source (FOPEN, buffer $FC00) and a destination (FWOPEN) open. If FGETB/FPUTB operate on independent handles, the write file may never be flushed/closed, risking truncated output on the last buffer. UNVERIFIED: hinges on the BIOS file-handle/close contract, which is not in this file. → Check the FCLOSE/FWOPEN ABI; if write files need their own close/flush, add it. Confirm the single-close mirrors the tested C version's behavior.
- _low_ · isa-misuse · cf_cp inner loop, lines 459-461 — P1 is set to $0000 (LDA #0/TAP1L/TAP1H) immediately before FPUTB, while the source read buffer was established at $FC00. The purpose of pointing P1 at $0000 is unclear and inconsistent with the read side; if FPUTB takes the byte in A and ignores P1, this is dead setup, and if it does use P1 it looks like the wrong address. UNVERIFIED without the FPUTB ABI. → Add a comment stating exactly what FPUTB expects in P1; if it ignores P1, delete the three-instruction setup.
- _low_ · efficiency · names_a +256 step, lines 990-992 (also cpw_g line 127; abspath ap_n) — Minor dead/wasted work: `LDA ht / LDB #0 / ADD / STA ht` adds zero to the low byte and is a no-op; the redundant `LDA (P2)` at cpw_g reloads a byte CMP already left in A; `ap_n` is maintained in abspath but never read. → Drop the +0 add, drop the redundant reload, and either use or remove ap_n. Individually trivial but they obscure intent in already-dense code.
- _low_ · safety · src-word copy (96-135), abspath (791-852) — patw (80), src/dst (80), and the abspath output have no length checks against input. cpw is an 8-bit counter with no cap, so an over-long path argument overflows patw into gfiles (or src/dst into neighbours) with no diagnostic. → Cap the copy loops at buffer size minus one and forcibly terminate, or reject over-length paths with the usage/error message.
- _low_ · clarity · c_dofile path (214-227) vs c_glob path (269-281) — The glob path joins the source basename onto the dst directory (joinp jdst,dst,basename), but the single-file path copies src->dst literally with no basename join. So `cp foo bar/` (bar an existing dir) behaves differently from the glob form `cp *oo bar/`. This is an easy-to-miss behavioral inconsistency versus typical `cp` semantics. → Decide intended semantics; if 'copy into a directory when dst is a dir' is wanted, apply the same isdir(dst)+basename-join in the single-file path (or document the divergence).
- _low_ · correctness · SYS_DIRENTRY target `de` (used at 537,543,561,592) — no .fill in this file — `de` is referenced as the SYS_DIRENTRY output buffer but is not declared in the BSS block (1066-1112); labels are flat/global so it presumably comes from an included module (#use glob/globx/abi). If it is not actually provided there, SYS_DIRENTRY writes to an unresolved address. UNVERIFIED — the file evidently assembles as shipped, so `de` is most likely defined in a #use'd lib. → Confirm `de` (and its size, >=13 bytes for 12-char name + type) is defined by one of the included modules; if not, add a local `.fill 16` and a comment.

**Docs:** Strong file header (lines 1-13) and per-routine headers for copy_file, isdir, copy_tree, scopy, joinp, abspath, and the index helpers; the buffer-choice rationale ($FC00 read buffer, $E000 dir buffer above the enlarged binary) and the "buffer the whole listing before recursing" invariant are well explained. Two problems: (1) the copy_tree header (lines 494-495) says 'names >=24 chars are skipped', but the code at 549-551 actually caps the ENTRY COUNT at 24 (nn_a returns the per-level entry counter, not a name length) and separately truncates each name to 12 chars — the comment conflates two different limits and describes neither correctly. (2) The 8-level depth cap, the 24-entries-per-directory cap, and the 12-char name truncation are stated as array sizes but never flagged as silent-failure limits. `ap_n` is computed and documented as a length but never consumed.

**Strengths:** Recursion state is managed correctly and deliberately: each copy_tree level immediately strcpy's ct_s/ct_d into per-depth sp[d]/dp[d] before the shared global jsrc/jdst buffers get overwritten by child joinp calls, and cti/ctk are reloaded/reset after every recursion so their clobbering by children is harmless.; Every carry branch (JC/JNC) after CMP/SUB/ADD that I traced is on the correct side — the count>=24 skip (JC), glob i>=cnt terminate (JC), FGETB EOF (JC), FOPEN/FOPENDIR failure (JC), and all the multi-byte pointer-increment JNC skips.; The two-pass copy_tree design (fully buffer a directory's listing into names[d]/isd[d] before any recursion) correctly finesses the single shared FSDIRBUF/FNEXT global, and the buffer-address choices ($FC00 read, $E000 dir) are justified in the header against the enlarged-binary memory map.; Good documentation altitude for hand-asm: a real file header enumerating BIOS/OS entry points and the entry contract, plus per-routine headers explaining non-obvious ABI details (P1/P2 clobbers, why bytes are stashed in cfch).; Index helpers cleanly encapsulate the no-indexed-addressing offset math (mul80 shared by sp/dp, per-stride loops for isd/names), keeping the call sites readable.

**Top improvements:** Add an explicit w_depth bound check before recursing (medium) — the 8-level array cap is currently enforced only by hope; deeper trees corrupt adjacent BSS.; Resolve the 12-char name termination question against the actual dirent format and os/commands/cp.c, and guarantee a NUL in every names slot (medium).; Surface errors instead of swallowing them: report dst-open/write failures in copy_file and propagate copy_file's status through the glob and tree loops; also warn (or handle) when a directory exceeds the 24-entry buffer.; Fix the misleading copy_tree header comment ('names >=24 chars are skipped') to describe the actual 24-entry-count cap and 12-char name truncation, and document the 8-level depth limit as a hard cap.


### `os/commands-asm/dep.asm`  
**Code: A  ·  Docs: A**  ·  P8X ASSEMBLY

Hand-coded /BIN `DEP` command: parses a hex address and a list of hex byte values from the arg tail at (P2) and pokes each byte into ascending memory. Quiet on success; prints usage on -h/empty and "dep: bad address" on a non-hex address.

This is a genuinely clean, correct piece of hand assembly. I traced every CMP/SUB flag branch, the 16-bit shift-and-accumulate in GETHEX, the 16-bit address increment in the main loop, and the P1/P2 pointer lifetimes across all JSRs, and found no correctness bugs — the carry/borrow branches are all oriented correctly, which is the hard part on this ISA. The code uses the ISA idiomatically (CMP-preserves-A range tests, SHL/ROL carry chaining for <<4, INPn for pointer walk) and documentation is above average. The only issues are minor: one stretch of effectively-dead carry-propagation code, silent truncation of over-range byte values, and a couple of undocumented ABI details.

**Findings:**

- _low_ · correctness · GETHEX gh_nc, lines 111-118 — After the 4-bit left shift, HXLO's low nibble is always 0, so `LDB DIGIT; ADD` (digit 0..15) can never carry out of the low byte (max 0xF0+0x0F=0xFF). The `JNC gh_nc / INC` carry-propagation into HXHI is therefore dead code — it never executes. → Either drop the carry test and just `STA HXLO` after the ADD (HXHI is already correct from the ROL chain), or replace `ADD` with `OR DIGIT` to make the 'merge digit into cleared nibble' intent explicit and self-evidently carry-free. Not a bug, but it misleads a reader into thinking cross-byte carry matters here.
- _low_ · correctness · d_lp store, lines 51-53 — Each value is parsed as a full 16-bit number in HXLO/HXHI but only HXLO is stored, so an over-range 'byte' like 100 (hex) or 1FF is silently truncated to its low 8 bits with no diagnostic. Whether this matches os/commands/dep.c was not verified. → Confirm the C counterpart's behavior; if it rejects values >0xFF, add a check on HXHI after the value GETHEX and branch to d_bad (or a 'bad value' message). If truncation is intended, document it in the header. Marked low/unverified pending a diff against dep.c.
- _low_ · docs · d_bad/d_use, lines 68 and 77 — `LDA #0` is loaded immediately before each `JSR SYS_PUTS` with no comment; its role in the SYS_PUTS ABI is opaque to a reader. → Add a short inline comment naming the argument (e.g. '; A=0: no length limit / stdout') consistent with the ABI docs referenced by //#use abi.
- _low_ · clarity · HEXVAL, lines 143-160 and 168-176 — On the non-lowercase path A already holds the original char (CMP preserves A), yet HVT is reloaded before each range test and before each SUB. The reloads are required only on the lowercase-converted path, so on the common path several `LDA HVT` are redundant. → Optional micro-optimization only — keeping the uniform `LDA HVT` reloads is arguably clearer than tracking when A is still live, so this is fine to leave. Noted for completeness, not worth changing.
- _low_ · correctness · d_lp / d_done, lines 45-47 — `dep 8000` with an address but zero byte values parses the address, finds no value (MATCH=0), and returns quietly having poked nothing. This may be intended ('quiet on success') but could also be a usage error the user would want flagged. → Confirm against dep.c; if a no-op DEP should warn, track whether any byte was written and emit usage when none. Otherwise document the no-op-is-success behavior.

**Docs:** Strong for hand-asm: a real file header (lines 1-5) describing behavior and entry contract, a main-entry block comment that even lists register/scratch clobbers (9-12), and accurate per-routine headers for GETHEX (83-84) and HEXVAL (133-135). The HEXVAL header correctly explains the one-past-the-end boundary constants ($3A, $47) and the CMP C-flag convention — inline comments match the code throughout. Gaps: (1) the `LDA #0` immediately before every `JSR SYS_PUTS` is unexplained (looks like an ABI arg — length/fd?) and deserves a one-word comment; (2) nothing documents that only the low byte (HXLO) of each parsed value is stored, so a value like "1FF" silently becomes $FF; (3) the file-end scratch cells have no collective comment noting they live in writable TPA RAM (fine because .org $6A00, but worth a line).

**Strengths:** Every carry/borrow branch is correct — the JC/JNC after each CMP in HEXVAL's range tests, the 16-bit address increment (lines 54-62), and the shift-accumulate are all oriented the right way, which is the most bug-prone part of this ISA.; Idiomatic ISA use: relies on CMP-preserves-A for chained range checks, uses SHL+ROL to carry-chain a 16-bit <<4, and walks the arg tail with INP2 rather than manual pointer math.; P1/P2 discipline is clean: HEXVAL touches only A/B/scratch (never the pointers), GETHEX owns P2, and P1 is (re)loaded from ADLO/ADHI right before each poke — no cross-call pointer clobbers.; Clever, well-documented boundary handling in HEXVAL using one-past-the-end constants ($3A after '9', $47 after 'F') plus a lowercase-fold, keeping the digit classifier compact.; Documentation is well above typical hand-asm: file header, clobber list, and accurate per-routine headers whose comments match the code.

**Top improvements:** Remove or convert the dead carry-propagation in GETHEX (lines 111-118) — replace `ADD DIGIT` with `OR DIGIT` and drop the JNC/INC, making the merge self-evidently carry-free.; Decide and document the over-range byte policy (store-low-byte vs. reject): either add an HXHI check before the poke or note the truncation in the header, verified against os/commands/dep.c.; Add a one-line comment explaining the `LDA #0` SYS_PUTS argument at lines 68 and 77.


### `os/commands-asm/diff.asm`  
**Code: B  ·  Docs: A**  ·  P8X ASSEMBLY (8-BIT TTL ISA)

Hand-coded DIFF command: loads two text files (<=96 lines x 79 chars) into fixed 80-byte-per-line grids, trims the common leading/trailing lines, and prints the differing middle region tagged "< " (file1) and "> " (file2). Includes an inline abspath builder.

Solid, careful hand-assembly. The carry/borrow branch directions after CMP are correct everywhere I checked (the flagged bug class is absent here), the JSR/RTS nesting is balanced with no manual stack juggling, and the prefix/suffix trimming logic is sound including the empty-region and unequal-length edge cases. Documentation is genuinely excellent. The main weaknesses are an O(n^2) address recomputation in the byte-compare hot path and a couple of unverified ISA-behavior assumptions worth confirming against the firmware. No confirmed correctness bug.

**Findings:**

- _medium_ · efficiency · leq / le_l loop (408-443), laddr calls at 418 & 429 — laddr recomputes base + line*80 (via a repeated-add multiply loop that iterates line-index times) for BOTH lines on EVERY byte comparison. Comparing line N costs ~N add-iterations per byte, twice — quadratic in line index. loadlines (ll_rd, line 353) has the same shape, recomputing lln*80 per character. → Compute each row base once (call laddr with col 0, or inline lln*80) and then advance the pointer with INP1 across the byte loop instead of recomputing the full address per byte. Bounded by the 96-line/79-char caps so not fatal, but it is the dominant cost of the whole command.
- _low_ · isa-misuse · laddr, line 520 (LDA #0 between ADD at 518 and JNC at 521) — The base low-byte add materializes its carry with the idiom `ADD / STA lat / LDA #0 / JNC lad_3 / LDA #1`. The JNC at 521 must reflect the ADD at 518, so this only works if `LDA #0` does not disturb the carry flag. This is the ONLY carry test in the file with an intervening LDA (elsewhere JNC immediately follows STA). UNVERIFIED — depends on LDA not touching C. The whole file assumes this, so it is almost certainly correct, but it is the one spot where the assumption is load-bearing and non-obvious. → Confirm against the firmware header that LDA leaves C untouched. If it does, add a one-line comment here noting the idiom relies on it; if not, capture the carry before the LDA.
- _low_ · correctness · emit, et_l (455-461) and el_l (471-477) — The tag/line print loops do `LDA (P1) / JSR SYS_PUTC / INP1`, relying on SYS_PUTC preserving P1 across the call. Note the asymmetry: openf documents FRESOLVE/FOPEN as clobbering P1/P2, and abspath explicitly reloads P1 after SYS_GETCWD (558-561) — yet emit trusts SYS_PUTC to keep P1. UNVERIFIED — if the syscall ABI clobbers general pointers, tag/line printing breaks. Since GETCWD writes through P1 it would need reloading regardless, so this is consistent with SYS_PUTC preserving pointers, and the command presumably passes its tests. → Verify SYS_PUTC's pointer-preservation contract in the syscall ABI doc; if pointers are not guaranteed preserved, spill/restore P1 (or hold the cursor in a scratch var) around each SYS_PUTC.
- _low_ · safety · abspath (542) writing into `path` (.fill 80, line 647) — abspath copies CWD + '/' + token into the 80-byte `path` buffer with no length check. A long CWD plus a long token can overrun `path` into the adjacent `alines` grid (648). Bounded in practice by short caps and typical paths, but there is no guard. → Cap the copy at the buffer size, or document the assumption that CWD+token < 80. Low risk given the environment but worth a note.
- _low_ · clarity · d_chk option handling (40-57) — On a leading '-' that is not -h/-H (e.g. "-x file2", or a bare "-"), control falls through to d_f1 while d_arg still points at the '-'. abspath then copies "-x" as a filename token, which fails to resolve and yields "file1 not found" rather than a usage message. Probably matches the C version, but the behavior is non-obvious. → If intended, add a comment that unknown options are treated as filenames; otherwise emit usage for any leading '-'.
- _low_ · efficiency · d_sk (21-37), d_f1s (76-92) — Space-skipping advances the arg pointer by loading d_arg, doing a manual 16-bit `ADD #1` with explicit high-byte carry, then reloading P2 from d_arg every iteration. This is what INP2 exists for. → Keep the working cursor in P2 and use INP2 to advance, spilling to d_arg only across calls that clobber P2. Removes the per-iteration manual increment and reload.
- _low_ · docs · leq header, line 407 — Clobber list omits the la* scratch vars (la_base/la_s/la_c/lat/lan/lacar) that leq destroys via its two JSR laddr calls. → Add "(and la* via laddr)" to the leq Clobbers line for accuracy.

**Docs:** Outstanding for hand asm: a real file header (algorithm, memory model, syscall/BIOS addresses, entry contract), and per-routine headers with In/Out/Clobbers. Inline comments correctly restate the carry semantics at each CMP branch (e.g. line 137, 143) and they match the code. One gap: the leq header (line 407) lists "Clobbers P1, le_i/a/b" but omits that leq also clobbers all the la* scratch vars via its JSR laddr calls — harmless in current callers but the clobber list is incomplete. Otherwise accurate and sufficient.

**Strengths:** Every CMP-based carry/borrow branch is in the correct direction (JC for A>=B on the >= tests at pfx_l, sfx checks, de_a/de_b, and the 96/79 caps) — the file avoids the flagged inverted-carry bug class entirely.; Prefix/suffix trimming handles the tricky edges correctly: suffix never crosses the prefix (dsa/dsb stop at dp), and the identical-files case returns cleanly with no output.; Documentation is exemplary for hand asm — file-level algorithm/memory-model header plus per-routine In/Out/Clobbers, and inline comments that accurately restate flag semantics at each branch.; Clean structure: no manual stack manipulation, balanced JSR/RTS, no dead code, and magic numbers (80/96/79/$FC00) are consistently used and explained.; Author is already carry-aware in multi-byte arithmetic (laddr's lacar chaining, the 16-bit pointer bumps), showing correct handling of the no-add-with-carry ISA constraint.

**Top improvements:** Hoist the row-base address computation out of the per-byte loops in leq (and loadlines) — compute the line base once and walk with INP1 — to remove the quadratic laddr recomputation.; Confirm and document two ISA/ABI assumptions: that LDA preserves carry (laddr line 520) and that SYS_PUTC preserves P1 (emit loops); add short comments once verified.; Add a length guard (or an explicit documented assumption) for the 80-byte `path` buffer in abspath to prevent CWD+token overrun into alines.; Fix the leq clobber-list comment to include the la* scratch vars destroyed via laddr.


### `os/commands-asm/dir.asm`  
**Code: B  ·  Docs: B**  ·  P8X ASSEMBLY

Hand-coded DIR command: lists a directory (or single file / glob) with a 6-wide size column, optional -R recursion and -S size sort, buffering entries into global arrays and selection-sorting an index array before printing. ASM counterpart of os/commands/dir.c.

This is genuinely solid, careful hand assembly. Every multi-byte carry/borrow chain I traced (divmod10, the 24-bit size compare in `before`, the pointer +offset helpers, arg_inc) uses the correct JC/JNC sense — I found no inverted-carry bugs, which is the dominant failure mode for this ISA. The recursion design (fully collect+print a level, recording child LBAs into per-depth csub[] before descending) is correct and lets the global entry buffers be safely reused at every depth; I verified the parent never re-reads a clobbered global after its print phase. gmatch's PHA/PLA save-restore is stack-balanced. The main weaknesses are non-correctness: two fully dead routines shipped in a tight TPA, an unguarded recursion depth that overflows the per-depth arrays on trees deeper than 16, and a few comments that slightly overstate or mislabel what the code does.

**Findings:**

- _medium_ · correctness · walk (w_depth increment ~line 463-465; nsub_addr/idx_addr/csub_addr 1428-1494) — Recursion depth is never bounded. nsub[]/idx[] are 16 bytes and csub[] is 2048 bytes = exactly 16 levels x 128. At w_depth==16 (a directory tree 17 levels deep) nsub_addr/idx_addr index one past their arrays (into the neighboring array) and csub_addr computes offset 16*128=2048, one past csub[] — silent memory corruption, not a clean error. → Before `JSR walk` in the descent (and/or at walk entry) compare w_depth against a MAXDEPTH (e.g. 16) and skip descending / return if reached, mirroring whatever cap dir.c enforces. I did NOT confirm dir.c's behavior, so verify the C side actually guards this before treating it as a divergence.
- _medium_ · efficiency · putnum (1181-1230), ndigits (1235-1259); storage pn/pncnt/ndv/ndc (1607-1610) — putnum and ndigits are dead code — no JSR targets them anywhere; the live size column uses the 24-bit putsize/dm10 path. They plus their scratch (pn, pncnt, ndv, ndc) consume code+data bytes in the ~37.9K TPA for no benefit. → Delete putnum, ndigits, and their exclusive scratch, or if intentionally retained as a shared helper library, move them out and note why. At minimum drop the misleading '(Helper.)' framing.
- _low_ · efficiency · name_before (735) via enam_ptr -> amul12 (902, 947) — The selection sort is O(n^2) comparisons; each name comparison calls enam_ptr twice, and enam_ptr computes mi*12 via amul12's repeated-add loop (up to 63 iterations). Net address arithmetic is roughly O(n^3) adds for n up to 64. → Either precompute each entry's enam base pointer once into a small table indexed by entry number, or replace amul12's repeated-add with mi*8 + mi*4 (two shifts + add) to cut the inner cost. Correctness is fine; this is purely cycles on a slow CPU.
- _low_ · correctness · col_nx dot check (494-497) — `LDA de; LDB #'.'; CMP; JZ col_nx` skips EVERY entry whose first byte is '.', not just "." and ".." as the comment claims. Any file named like ".cfg" would be hidden. → If the filesystem's 12-byte name field can ever hold a leading-dot name, tighten to reject only "." and ".." (check that byte1 is '.' or pad). If dir.c does the same broad check and dotfiles cannot exist in this FS, leave the code and just correct the comment. Marked low/unverified pending the dir.c and name-format check.
- _low_ · safety · dbuf copy (242-260, dbuf .fill 64), gpat copy (214-232, .fill 16), apath (abspath, .fill 80) — The path-split copies into dbuf/gpat and the CWD-prefix into apath have no length guard. A path component longer than the buffer (or a very long CWD) overruns into adjacent scratch. → Bound each copy loop by the buffer size (compare cnt against the .fill size and truncate), matching whatever limit the OS shell already imposes on the arg tail. Low because normal shell input is short.
- _low_ · clarity · eidx_get/eisd_get/elen2_get (866-899), nsub_addr/idx_addr (1428-1447), and the inline base+index sequences in collect/sort — The `LDA #<base; LDB idx; ADD; TAP?L; LDA #>base; JNC .+; INC; TAP?H` pattern is open-coded ~15 times, several of them inline rather than via the existing helpers, which invites a copy/paste carry-branch slip in future edits. → If the assembler supports macros, factor a single `PTR base,idx` macro; otherwise route the inline sites (e.g. the eisd/elen2/eidx stores in collect and the sort swap at 657-676) through the existing *_get/*_addr helpers where the index is already in a byte.
- _low_ · clarity · d_opt flag loop (64-103) — Combined flags like `-RS` are not supported: after handling -R, arg_inc skips only '-' and 'R', leaving 'S', which d_opt then treats as the start of a path token. This matches the header's 'each its own -x token' note, so it is by design, but it is a silent surprise (`dir -RS` lists a path named 'S'). → No code change required; consider a one-line comment at d_oadv making explicit that the second arg_inc assumes a single flag char per token so combined flags are intentionally unsupported.

**Docs:** Strong file header (lines 1-25) accurately describes the algorithm, buffer reuse strategy, BIOS/OS entry points, and the 24-bit size handling. Most routines have a one-line header and the inline comments on the tricky spots (divmod10 byte-wise divide bound "<2560", enam_ptr "offset up to 756", the -h "first token only" note) are accurate and genuinely helpful. Deductions: (1) putnum/ndigits are labeled "(Helper.)" but are never called by any live path — the comment should say "currently unused" so a reader doesn't hunt for the caller; (2) the col_nx comment "skip '.'/'..'" understates the code, which skips every name beginning with '.', not just the two dot entries; (3) no routine documents the hard depth ceiling of 16 or that it is unchecked.

**Strengths:** Correct carry/borrow discipline throughout — divmod10's multi-byte subtract, the 24-bit descending compare in `before` (hi byte first, JZ-then-JC), amul12/csub_addr carry propagation, and every *_ptr helper use the right JC/JNC sense; no inverted branches found.; Recursion design is sound: each level is fully collected and printed (recording child LBAs into per-depth csub[]) before descending, so the shared global entry buffers can be reused at every depth without the parent ever reading stale data after its print phase.; Good ISA economy: post-increment (Pn)+ stores/loads, TAPnL/H pointer setup, and a correctly balanced PHA/PLA save-restore around the recursive '*' branch in gmatch (4 pushes, 4 pops per iteration, shared exit labels all balanced).; Thorough, mostly-accurate documentation: a real file header explaining the algorithm and buffer layout, plus load-bearing inline notes (divmod bound <2560, dv/dvq single-byte safety, the -h first-token rule, the assembler ';'-in-string workaround for the usage message).; Careful 24-bit size handling end to end (elen2/de[17], skey, the compare, and the byte-wise dm10 printer), with the zero-size and dir-as-size-0 edge cases handled correctly.

**Top improvements:** Remove the dead putnum/ndigits routines and their exclusive scratch (pn/pncnt/ndv/ndc) to reclaim TPA space, or relocate them to a real shared helper module.; Add an explicit recursion-depth guard (MAXDEPTH 16) before descending so deep trees error cleanly instead of overrunning nsub[]/idx[]/csub[].; Fix the two comment inaccuracies: the col_nx dot-skip (all leading-dot names, not just './..') and the '(Helper.)' label on the unused routines.; Cut the sort's address-math cost by precomputing per-entry enam pointers (or replacing amul12's repeated add with shift-add), and factor the repeated base+index pointer boilerplate into a macro/helper.


### `os/commands-asm/disasm.asm`  
**Code: B  ·  Docs: B**  ·  P8X ASSEMBLY

Hand-coded DISASM /BIN command: parses two hex addresses and disassembles the half-open memory range [start,end), decoding each byte via the generated DISTAB opcode table. It is the ASM counterpart of os/commands/disasm.c and aims for byte-identical output.

This is careful, correct hand assembly. I traced every carry/borrow branch, the 16-bit compare/add chains, the operand-shape routing, and the DISTAB scan, and cross-checked all output paths against disasm.c — the two genuinely match, including the little-endian ph4 ordering, the raw-byte padding column, and the CRLF-vs-LF distinction between instruction lines and usage messages. I found no correctness bugs. The main weaknesses are missed register economy (the ISA guarantees CMP preserves A, yet the code reloads SHAPE/HVT after every compare), one misleading routine comment about register usage, and a little dead/hardcoded code. Solid work that leaves some cycles and bytes on the table.

**Findings:**

- _medium_ · docs · PRS, lines 360-361 — The routine header states PRS uses P2 as the cursor save and re-points P2 each character. The implementation instead saves the pointer in the PSL/PSH globals (TPA1L->PSL, TPA1H->PSH) and reconstructs P1 from them; P2 is never read or written. The comment misdescribes register liveness, which is exactly the kind of thing a maintainer relies on when reasoning about clobbers. → Fix the comment to say the cursor is saved in PSL/PSH (because syscalls clobber P1). Optionally note that P2 is free here because the arg tail is no longer needed after parsing.
- _medium_ · efficiency · CALC_LN (180-191), DS_OPER (206-224), do_preg (276-282), HEXVAL (469-490) — The ISA guarantees CMP preserves A, and LDB does not touch A, so after 'LDA SHAPE / LDB #n / CMP' the accumulator still holds SHAPE. Yet the code reloads 'LDA SHAPE' before every subsequent compare (3x in CALC_LN, ~4x in DS_OPER, 2x in do_preg), and similarly reloads 'LDA HVT' in HEXVAL where A is already the folded value. Each redundant load is wasted bytes and cycles in hot paths. → Drop the redundant reloads and chain compares directly, e.g. in CALC_LN: LDA SHAPE / LDB #1 / CMP / JZ cl_2 / LDB #2 / CMP / JZ cl_3 / LDB #9 / CMP / JZ cl_5 / .... Verify output is unchanged (it will be, since CMP preserves A).
- _low_ · clarity · do_preg, line 289 — 'JSR $2009' hardcodes the SYS_PUTC address instead of using the SYS_PUTC label used everywhere else in the file. If the ABI address ever moves, this call silently breaks while all others follow the equate. The comment acknowledges it but does not justify it. → Replace with 'JSR SYS_PUTC' for consistency and ABI-safety.
- _low_ · clarity · d_puts, line 173 — 'LDA #0' immediately precedes 'JSR SYS_PUTS'. SYS_PUTS (verified in p8xos.asm: 'LDA (P1)+ ...') ignores A and overwrites it on the first instruction, so the load is dead. It reads as if it sets up an argument, which is misleading. → Remove the LDA #0, or if it is intended as documentation that SYS_PUTS takes its string in P1, replace with a comment.
- _low_ · efficiency · W16OFF, lines 314-315 — 'STA TMPB' followed immediately by 'LDA TMPB' is redundant: STA does not modify A, so A still holds the offset. The reload wastes an instruction. → Delete line 315 (LDA TMPB); A already contains the offset for the following JSR PEEKAT.
- _low_ · style · TMPA declaration, line 520 — TMPA is declared '.fill 2' but is only ever used as a single byte (STA TMPA in PEEKAT/DIS_FIND, LDB TMPA). The extra byte is wasted and slightly misleading about its width. → Change to '.fill 1' to match usage (matches TMPB just below).
- _low_ · correctness · DIS_FIND, lines 330-333 — The table scan treats a record opcode byte of $FF as the end-of-table sentinel. This is correct only as long as no real opcode is $FF; if a future genucode.OPC ever assigns opcode $FF, DIS_FIND would treat it as end-of-table and mis-report that instruction as unknown. The C version is immune because it iterates by dt_n count rather than a sentinel. Currently safe (no $FF opcode in lib_distab.inc); flagged as a latent fragility, unverified against any planned opcode changes. → Document the invariant ('$FF must never be a real opcode') near the sentinel check, or have the generator assert it, so the coupling is explicit.

**Docs:** Strong and unusually thorough: a real file header explaining provenance/output format, a header comment on every routine, and accurate inline notes on the non-obvious parts (the unsigned 16-bit compare, the pad column, the $37 nibble bias, the '-h' handling). Two blemishes: the PRS header (lines 360-361) claims it "Uses P2 as a save ... copy the cursor to P2 and re-point each char," but the code actually saves to the PSL/PSH globals and never touches P2 — a misleading statement about register liveness, which matters most in asm. Minor: the top comment says "puts = string + LF"; that is fine, but a reader must know SYS_PUTS itself appends nothing (verified in p8xos.asm) for it to make sense.

**Strengths:** All carry/borrow branches are correct — the unsigned 16-bit addr<end compare (d_loop: JNC/JNZ on the high byte, JC on the low byte) and both 16-bit addr+=LN chains (ADD then JNC/INC) are textbook-correct, avoiding the classic inverted-JC bug this ISA invites.; Faithful, verified mirror of disasm.c: operand-shape routing, instruction lengths, the fixed-width raw-byte pad column, the little-endian ph4 (WH before WL) ordering, and the CRLF on instruction lines vs single-LF on usage/error messages all match the C source exactly.; Clean subroutine factoring (PEEKAT, W16OFF, DIS_FIND, PRS, OPH8/ONIB, GETHEX, HEXVAL) with a flat memory-variable calling convention that keeps the shallow JSR nesting stack-balanced; every JSR path has a matching RTS, and tail-call JMPs (OPH8->ONIB) are used correctly.; Genuinely good documentation discipline for hand asm: a provenance-explaining file header plus a header on every routine, and accurate inline reasoning for the subtle bits (the $37 nibble bias mapping 10..15 to 'A'..'F', the case-fold ranges in HEXVAL, the shape->register pairing).; Correct 16-bit hex accumulation in GETHEX via SHL(HXLO)+ROL(HXHI) repeated four times then add-with-carry — the shift-through-carry semantics are used exactly right.

**Top improvements:** Exploit 'CMP preserves A' to delete the redundant LDA SHAPE / LDA HVT reloads in CALC_LN, DS_OPER, do_preg, and HEXVAL — the single highest-value change for size and speed with zero behavioral risk.; Fix the PRS header comment to reflect that it saves the cursor in PSL/PSH (not P2), so the register-liveness note is accurate.; Replace the hardcoded 'JSR $2009' at line 289 with 'JSR SYS_PUTC', and remove the dead 'LDA #0' at line 173 and the redundant 'LDA TMPB' at line 315.; Make the $FF end-of-table invariant explicit (comment or generator assertion) so DIS_FIND's sentinel assumption is documented.


### `os/commands-asm/dump.asm`  
**Code: B  ·  Docs: A**  ·  ASM

Hand-coded DUMP command: parses a hex address, prints 256-byte pages (16x16) as hex+ASCII, pauses per page ('.' quits).

Clean, well-structured, apparently correct given standard ISA assumptions. Main open risks are ISA-dependent (SYS_PUTC preserving P1; SHL/SHR fill-0 vs rotate-through-carry). Minor redundancies + one raw-hex call.

**Findings:**

- _low_ · efficiency · u_asc lines 80-90 — CMP preserves A, so the STA ATMP + two LDA ATMP reloads are redundant on this path. → Drop ATMP: LDA (P1)+ / LDB #32 / CMP / JNC u_dot / LDB #127 / CMP / JC u_dot / JMP u_put.
- _low_ · correctness · u_hex/u_asc loops read (P1)+ across JSR SYS_PUTC/OPH8 — Whole inner loop assumes P1 survives SYS_PUTC; project note records syscalls clobber P1/P2. Most load-bearing ISA assumption in the file. → Confirm SYS_PUTC preserves P1; else hold the row ptr in ADLO/ADHI and reload.
- _low_ · isa-misuse · OPH8 four SHRs; GETHEX gh_shl SHL then ROL — Bare shifts with no carry-clear; if SHR/SHL rotate through carry a stray carry leaks into the result. Correct only if logical (0-fill). → Verify SHR/SHL fill 0; else clear carry first / clarify the header.
- _low_ · style · u_put line 92 JSR $2009 — Raw address instead of the SYS_PUTC equate used elsewhere; violates the named-address convention. → Use JSR SYS_PUTC.

**Docs:** Excellent header (usage, entry convention, BIOS/SYS addrs, scratch layout, ISA carry primer); inline comments accurate and explanatory. Blemishes: muddled u_asc comment rationalizing redundant reloads; raw $2009 call inconsistent with the file's own SYS_PUTC equate.

**Strengths:** JC/JNC polarity correct throughout incl. two-sided printable-range test; Hand-rolled 16-bit add/shift correct with proper carry propagation; Balanced JSR/RTS; OPH8 tail-calls ONIB (documented); Unusually good docs (ISA primer + per-block rationale); Clean reusable helpers with stated in/out/clobber contracts

**Top improvements:** Confirm P1 survives SYS_PUTC; reload from ADLO/ADHI if not; Remove redundant ATMP store/reloads (CMP preserves A); Nail down SHR/SHL carry behavior; use SYS_PUTC equate not raw $2009


### `os/commands-asm/examine.asm`  
**Code: B  ·  Docs: A**  ·  P8X ASSEMBLY

Hand-coded EXAMINE shell command: parses a hex address argument, then interactively displays and optionally rewrites memory one byte at a time (Enter=advance, two hex digits=write, '.'=quit). ASM counterpart of os/commands/examine.c.

This is a well-crafted, idiomatic piece of hand assembly. I traced every routine (argument scan, GETHEX, HEXVAL, OPH8/ONIB, the 16-bit increment, and the interactive loop) and found no confirmed correctness bug: carry branches are oriented correctly for the stated CMP/SUB semantics (C=1 when A>=B), the 16-bit address increment and the GETHEX shift-accumulate chain are correct, HEXVAL's case-folding and range gates cover the '9'..'A' gap and all out-of-range chars, and the OPH8->ONIB tail-call is a clean, deliberate optimization. P1 is reloaded fresh before every dereference so syscall P1/P2 clobbering is a non-issue. The remaining notes are minor: a few redundant reloads that rely on (correct) CMP-preserves-A behavior, two duplicated print-and-return epilogues, and a couple of robustness gaps around EOF and address overflow.

**Findings:**

- _low_ · efficiency · lines 63,67,71 (main loop, ECHR) — The key is stored to ECHR then reloaded with LDA ECHR at lines 67 and 71 before each subsequent compare. Since the ISA header states CMP preserves A and no instruction between these points clobbers A, the two reloads (and arguably the ECHR variable itself) are dead work. → Drop the LDA ECHR reloads and compare against the value already in A: STA ECHR (if you still want it saved) then LDB #'.'/CMP/JZ, LDB #13/CMP/JZ, then fall straight into HEXVAL with A intact. Verify only that nothing between reintroduces a clobber.
- _low_ · correctness · e_cr (line 100) — After Enter, JSR SYS_GETC is used to swallow the queued LF but its return/carry is ignored. If input ends immediately after the CR (e.g. piped/redirected input with no trailing LF), this GETC hits EOF (C=1) and the byte is discarded, yet control still falls into e_next and loops once more before the next EOF is detected. Unverified against real SYS_GETC EOF timing. → Consider JC e_end after the LF-swallow GETC to exit cleanly on EOF, mirroring the JC guards at lines 62 and 78. Low impact for interactive terminal use.
- _low_ · correctness · GETHEX / gh_shl (lines 179-198) — An address with more than 4 hex digits silently overflows: the shift chain drops HXHI bit7 on each ROL and the digit-add carry past the high byte is lost, so e.g. '12345' parses as $2345 with MATCH=1 rather than being rejected. Behavior is intentional-looking (matches monitor leniency) but undocumented. → Either document that only the low 16 bits are kept, or set MATCH=0 / a separate overflow flag when GCNT exceeds 4 so 'bad address' is reported.
- _low_ · clarity · e_bad (120-128) and e_use (129-137) — The two error exits are byte-for-byte identical except the message pointer: load <msg/>msg into P1, LDA #0, JSR SYS_PUTS, LDA #10, JSR SYS_PUTC, RTS. Duplicated epilogue. → Factor a shared 'print P1 string then LF then RTS' tail (e.g. e_puts_ln), and have e_bad/e_use only set the pointer before jumping to it. Saves bytes and keeps the newline behavior in one place.
- _low_ · isa-misuse · e_next (110-112) and gh_nc (195-197) — Both 16-bit increments load the high byte (LDA ADHI / LDA HXHI) between the low-byte ADD and the JNC, relying on LDA not disturbing the carry flag. This is almost certainly correct for this ISA (the pattern is used consistently and the code ships tested byte-identical) but the assumption is load-bearing and unstated. → Add a one-line comment noting 'LDA preserves C' at one of these sites, or confirm against docs/p8x-system-design.md. Flagged as unverified, not a bug.

**Docs:** Excellent. A clear file header states purpose, per-byte UI semantics, entry convention (P2=arg tail), and the subtle SYS_GETC echo/queued-LF behavior that drives the swallow-LF logic. Every helper (OPH8, ONIB, GETHEX, HEXVAL) has an accurate one-line contract naming its inputs/outputs and clobbers. Inline comments are accurate against the code and explain the non-obvious bits (the <<4 nibble merge, ROL-pulls-carry in the 16-bit shift, the 10+$37='A' trick, the '9'+1/'F'+1 range fences). Magic constants ($37, $3A, $47, $20) are each annotated. Only gap: no comment records the load-does-not-affect-carry assumption the 16-bit adds depend on.

**Strengths:** No confirmed correctness bug: every carry/borrow branch (JC/JNC after CMP) matches the stated C=1-when-A>=B semantics, including the 16-bit address increment and the GETHEX shift-and-add accumulator.; OPH8 -> ONIB tail call (JMP ONIB, letting ONIB's RTS return to OPH8's caller) is a clean, correct byte/cycle saver rather than an accidental fall-through.; HEXVAL is robust: lowercase folding is gated to exactly 'a'..'f', and the range fences ($3A after '0', $47 after 'A') correctly reject the '9'..'A' gap and everything past 'F'.; P1 is reloaded from ADLO/ADHI immediately before each (P1) access, so the documented syscall P1/P2 clobbering cannot corrupt the pointer.; Documentation is thorough and accurate at both file and routine level, including the non-obvious SYS_GETC echo/queued-LF interaction that motivates the swallow-LF path.

**Top improvements:** Remove the redundant LDA ECHR reloads in the key-dispatch section (and reconsider the ECHR scratch var) now that CMP preserves A.; Factor the identical e_bad/e_use print-string-plus-LF epilogue into one shared tail routine.; Decide and document the >4-digit address behavior in GETHEX (reject vs. silently truncate to 16 bits).; Add a JC EOF guard on the LF-swallow GETC in e_cr, and a short comment recording the 'LDA preserves carry' assumption the 16-bit adds depend on.


### `os/commands-asm/find.asm`  
**Code: B  ·  Docs: A**  ·  P8X ASSEMBLY

Hand-coded FIND command: recursively walks the current working directory and prints every path whose final component matches a pattern (glob when '*'/'?' present, else substring). It is the asm counterpart of os/commands/find.c and shares gmatch/de[] via ;#use glob.

This is a carefully written, well-structured piece of hand assembly. The hard parts — 16-bit pointer arithmetic, manual carry chaining, and no-MUL strided addressing into per-level arrays — are all done correctly and consistently, with no inverted carry branches that I could find. The recursion-via-hardware-stack + depth-indexed-arrays design is clean and the root "/" double-slash special case is handled identically in both the build and print paths. The two real weaknesses are a missing runtime guard on recursion depth (silent memory corruption past MAXD) and one self-contradictory clobber comment. Documentation is unusually good for hand asm.

**Findings:**

- _medium_ · safety · walk (w_depth increment, lines 334-340) / array sizing lines 734-738 — There is no runtime guard preventing w_depth from reaching/exceeding MAXD (10). On a directory tree deeper than 10 levels, w_depth++ keeps going and the index helpers (nsub_a/idx_a/parr_a/clba_a/cn_a) compute addresses past the ends of nsub/idx/parr/clba/cn, silently corrupting adjacent TPA memory. The header comment even acknowledges this ('guarding recursion depth is the caller's dir-tree shape'). → Add a MAXD equate and, before the descend at line 334, compare w_depth+1 to MAXD (CMP; JC skip-descend) so deep trees are truncated safely instead of corrupting memory. At minimum, document the hard cap in the usage/HELP text.
- _medium_ · docs · print_match header, line 368 — Comment claims SYS_PUTC 'clobbers P1/P2 via syscall', but pm_l (376-386) and pm_nl (402-408) rely on P1 surviving across SYS_PUTC. If the comment were accurate the command would print garbage after the first character; since it evidently works, the comment is wrong. A future maintainer trusting it could 'fix' working code or wrongly reload P1 each iteration. → Correct the note to state that SYS_PUTC preserves P1/P2 (unlike the FS syscalls, which the code reloads after). Unverified against firmware source — if SYS_PUTC genuinely does clobber P1/P2, then pm_l/pm_nl are broken and need P1 saved/restored (PHW/PLW) around each call; worth confirming in the syscall table.
- _low_ · correctness · w_nomatch subdir recording, lines 201-205 — When a level already has 24 recorded children, additional subdirectories at that level are silently dropped (JC w_next). This means FIND will not descend into the 25th+ subdirectory of any directory, producing incomplete results with no warning. This is an intentional cap (commented) but is a genuine functional limitation, not just a perf choice. → Acceptable for a homebrew, but consider emitting a one-line 'find: too many entries, results truncated' diagnostic, or document the 24-children/level and 10-deep limits in the man page so users know results may be partial.
- _low_ · efficiency · cn_a (623-704) called per-character in wcn_l (217) and wnm_l (300) — cn_a recomputes the full strided address (w_depth*384 via 3x128 adds per level, plus fi*16 via a byte-at-a-time add loop) on every single character copied, inside the name-copy inner loops. For a name at level 9 this is ~30 adds per character just to derive the base pointer. → Compute the cn[d][fi] base pointer once before each copy loop and advance it with INP1 per character (the slots are contiguous 16-byte rows), the same way rdname/print_match already walk with INP1. Same optimization applies to clba access. Not a bug, just wasted cycles in the hot path.
- _low_ · clarity · scratch var block, lines 712-730 — A large pool of single-letter/opaque scratch names (fi, fk, fk2, fn, fch, fi2, cp, fpl, ft, fcar) are reused across many routines with overlapping lifetimes that the reader must trace by hand to confirm no aliasing (e.g. fpl doubles as the print-prefix length and as a general scratch in w_desc). This is the main readability cost of the file. → Group and comment the vars by owning routine, or give the load-bearing ones purpose names (e.g. plen_level, child_idx, name_idx). At least annotate which routines legitimately share each temp so future edits don't introduce an aliasing bug.

**Docs:** Excellent by hand-asm standards: a real file header explaining the data model (per-level arrays indexed by w_depth, hardware-stack recursion), BIOS/OS address map, and entry convention; every section has a header block; nearly every magic number (48=24*2, 384=24*16, 128*3, de+12 type, de+15/16 LBA, cap 63/24) is explained. Inline comments track the code accurately. The one blemish: the print_match header (line 368) says "Uses SYS_PUTC (clobbers P1/P2 via syscall)", yet the pm_l and pm_nl loops walk cur[]/nm[] with P1 held live *across* SYS_PUTC calls (LDA (P1); JSR SYS_PUTC; INP1; loop). Both cannot be true — since the FS syscalls (GETCWD/DIRENTRY/FNEXT) are consistently followed by pointer reloads while SYS_PUTC never is, the code clearly assumes SYS_PUTC preserves P1/P2 and the parenthetical is misleading. Also, "MAXD=10" appears only in comments; there is no named equate and no code enforces it.

**Strengths:** Manual 16-bit carry chaining is correct throughout — every low-byte ADD is followed by a JNC/INC high-byte fixup, and the base-address adds in clba_a/cn_a correctly capture the low-byte carry into fcar before adding the high byte. No inverted JC/JNC branches found.; The no-MUL strided addressing into per-level arrays (stride 48 for clba, 384 for cn, plus fi*2 via SHL and fi*16 via an add loop) is implemented cleanly and stays within the allocated buffers for the documented 10x24x16 bounds.; Consistent, disciplined syscall discipline: pointers are reloaded after every FS syscall (GETCWD/DIRENTRY/FNEXT/OPENDIR) that may clobber them.; The root-directory double-slash edge case (cur is exactly "/") is handled and, crucially, handled identically in both the path-building path (w_addsl) and the printing path (pm_sep), so built and printed paths agree.; Substring contains() correctly avoids buffer over-read: a mismatch against the haystack NUL terminator fails the inner loop rather than running past the string, and the empty-pattern case is unreachable because f_chk rejects empty/CR args.; Genuinely strong documentation: file-level data-model header, per-section headers, and explained magic numbers — rare and valuable for hand-written assembly.

**Top improvements:** Add a MAXD depth guard before the recursive descend (compare w_depth+1 to a MAXD equate) so directory trees deeper than 10 levels are truncated safely instead of overrunning the per-level arrays.; Fix the misleading 'SYS_PUTC clobbers P1/P2' comment in print_match (and verify against the syscall table); the code's correctness depends on P1 surviving PUTC.; Hoist the cn[]/clba[] base-pointer computation out of the per-character inner loops and walk with INP1, eliminating a large per-character addressing cost at deep levels.; Document (in the man page / usage) the hard limits — 10 levels deep, 24 subdirs per level, 63-char pattern — since exceeding them silently yields partial results.


### `os/commands-asm/grep.asm`  
**Code: C  ·  Docs: B**  ·  P8X ASSEMBLY

Hand-coded GREP /BIN command: prints input lines matching a basic regex (. * + ? ^ $), reading a file/glob/stdin, or with -r walking the CWD tree (find-style depth-indexed recursion) and printing "path:line". It is the asm counterpart of os/commands/grep.c.

A careful, largely faithful hand-port of grep.c. The CMP/JC carry directions I checked are all correct, pointer regs are reloaded after clobbering syscalls per the file's convention, and the 16-bit address arithmetic is chained correctly in most helpers. However there is one confirmed addressing bug: the cn[] per-depth stride helper computes 288 instead of the required 384, which corrupts subdir-name storage once the -r walk descends past depth 0 with many subdirs. There is also a real buffer overflow (line[] is 176 bytes but the scanner caps line length at 255, matching the same latent defect in grep.c). Documentation is thorough but contains two statements that are actively misleading. Grades reflect one confirmed functional bug plus a memory-corruption overflow against otherwise solid, well-structured code.

**Findings:**

- _high_ · correctness · cn_a (line 882, LDB #32) — The cn[] per-depth stride is computed as 288 bytes (INC ft+1 = +256 at lines 878-880, plus LDB #32/ADD = +32), but cn is declared cn[8][24][16] (line 982, .fill 3072) so the correct per-depth stride is 24*16 = 384. The C source confirms intent: collect() uses a per-frame local cn[384]. clba_a (the sibling helper) correctly strides 48/depth with no spurious INC. Result: for w_depth>=1, cn writes land 96 bytes low per level and overlap the parent depth's name table (depth-1 region [288,672) clobbers depth-0 cn[0][18..23]). During a -r walk of a tree whose subdirectories themselves contain subdirectories, subdir names for indices >=19 at an outer level get corrupted, so cur[]/the recorded rfiles path is wrong and those files are silently skipped or mislabeled. → Change LDB #32 to LDB #128 at line 882 (128 low + 256 high = 384). Verify against grep.c's cn[384] per-frame size and add a test with a directory that has >=19 subdirs one of which is itself nested.
- _high_ · safety · grep_stream overflow guard (lines 295-312) and decl line 975 — line[] is declared .fill 176 but the scanner only stops storing when gn >= 255 (LDB #255; CMP; JC gst_l), and gst_eol/gst_end NUL-terminate at line[gn]. Any input line longer than 175 chars writes line[176..255], overrunning into nm[], cur[], and the walk tables. This mirrors grep.c (char line[176] with `if (n < 255)`), so the defect exists in both and the two must stay in sync. → Either enlarge line[] to 256 bytes, or change the cap to 175 (LDB #175) so gn never exceeds the buffer. Apply the equivalent fix in os/commands/grep.c to keep the C and asm builds byte-consistent.
- _medium_ · safety · collect / w_depth increment (lines 580-586) — w_depth is incremented before each recursive collect() with no bound check, but the per-depth tables are sized for exactly 8 levels (nsub/gidxr/parr .fill 8; clba .fill 384=8*48; cn .fill 3072=8*384). A directory tree deeper than 8 levels indexes past every table (nsub_a/idx_a/parr_a/clba_a/cn_a all add w_depth unclamped) and corrupts adjacent RAM; deep JSR recursion also grows the P3 stack unbounded. → Guard the descent: if w_depth would reach 8, stop recursing (skip the subdir) rather than overflowing the tables. Document the hard depth limit at the collect header.
- _medium_ · safety · append_file (lines 610-714) / rfiles decl line 983 — rfiles[] slots are a fixed 80 bytes (rf_addr multiplies rfi*80), but the assembled path is cur[0..fpl) + '/' + nm + NUL, and cur[] is 176 bytes. A collected path longer than 79 chars overruns its slot into the next entry (or past rfiles for the last slot). No length check bounds the copy. Shared with grep.c (base = nrf*80). → Cap the path length while copying (stop/truncate at 79) or widen the slot, and mirror the guard in grep.c.
- _medium_ · docs · do_match header (line 353) — Comment asserts 'match/SYS_PUTC clobber P1/P2', but SYS_PUTC ($2009 -> OUTCH) provably preserves P1 and P2 on all three paths (OPUTS at os/p8xos.asm:4630 relies on it, and OUTFILE saves/restores P1, the capture path saves/restores P2). The dmp_l loop (lines 376-386) itself depends on P1 surviving SYS_PUTC. The comment is false for SYS_PUTC and could lead a maintainer to 'fix' the working loop or to wrongly trust the claim elsewhere. → Correct the comment to say match() clobbers P1/P2 but SYS_PUTC preserves them (which is why dmp_l may hold the prefix pointer in P1 across the call).
- _low_ · docs · cn_a header (lines 790-792) — The comment states cn's stride as '32 per depth', which merely restates the buggy LDB #32 rather than the geometry-correct 384 (24 subdirs * 16 bytes). It reinforces the wrong constant. → After fixing the stride, update the comment to 'stride 384 per depth (24 subdirs * 16 bytes), 16 per subdir'.
- _low_ · isa-misuse · clba_a (line 842, LDA fi / SHL) — cla_md is reached via JZ from `LDA fn; LDB #0; CMP` (fn==0), which sets C=1 (0>=0). The following `LDA fi; SHL` computes fi*2. If SHL rotates the carry flag into bit0 (rather than zero-filling), the index would become fi*2+1 and select the wrong LBA. UNVERIFIED: this depends on exact SHL microcode. It is probably a clean logical shift (a carry-in would break the common -r case that is presumably tested), but the code does not CLC first. → Confirm SHL zero-fills bit0 in the firmware/microcode; if it can rotate carry in, insert a CLC before the SHL. Low risk but worth a one-line confirmation or defensive CLC for clarity.
- _low_ · efficiency · g_sk (24-40), grs (76-92), gad_s/gad_s2 (142-158) — These space-skipping loops reload P2 from g_arg on every iteration even though nothing between iterations clobbers P2 (no syscall/match call inside the loop body). The reload is only needed once on entry. → Hoist the P2 reload out of the loop, or just advance P2 with INP2 alongside g_arg. Minor cycle/byte savings; not a correctness issue.
- _low_ · clarity · g_chk option parsing (lines 59-64) — '-H' is accepted as a help alias (line 59) but '-R' (uppercase) is not accepted as a recursion alias; '-R...' silently falls through and is treated as the regex. Minor asymmetry that could surprise users used to case-insensitive flags. → Either accept '-R' as recurse for symmetry, or document that flags are case-sensitive except -h/-H.

**Docs:** Strong file header (purpose, arg grammar, the two-phase -r rationale, entry contract P2=arg tail) and near-line-by-line routine comments; the .byte 59 semicolon-in-usage trick and the find-style flattening are well explained. But three comments are inaccurate or misleading: (1) line 353 "match/SYS_PUTC clobber P1/P2" is false for SYS_PUTC — OUTCH provably preserves both regs (OPUTS and grep's own dmp_l loop rely on it); (2) lines 790-792 describe cn's per-depth stride as "32 per depth", documenting the buggy constant rather than the intended 384; (3) line 975 "used up to 255" annotates a 176-byte buffer, documenting an overflow as if it were intended. Most helpers lack an explicit clobber/register contract, though do_match has one.

**Strengths:** Faithful, disciplined port of grep.c: control flow, phase split (collect then grep), and the find-style depth-indexed recursion are reproduced accurately.; All the carry/borrow branch directions I audited are correct: gi>=nrf (line 214), fi>=63 (108), gn>=255 (298), nrf>=36 (438), nsub>=24 (451) all use JC in the right sense after CMP.; Pointer discipline is consistent with the file's stated convention — P1/P2 are reloaded from RAM (g_arg, rfp, gs_pfx, etc.) after any match()/syscall that clobbers them.; 16-bit address math is carefully carry-chained (explicit rfcar/fcar high-byte carries in rf_addr, clba_a, cn_a; JNC/INC patterns for cur[]/line[] indexing).; Genuinely good documentation density: clear file header, per-routine intent comments, and a nicely explained workaround for embedding ';' in the usage string via .byte 59.; The dmp_l prefix loop correctly exploits SYS_PUTC preserving P1 (even though the nearby comment wrongly says otherwise).

**Top improvements:** Fix the cn_a per-depth stride: LDB #32 -> LDB #128 at line 882 so it strides 384 bytes/depth (confirmed against cn[8][24][16] and grep.c's cn[384]); add a >=19-subdir nested test.; Resolve the line[] overflow: size the buffer to 256 or cap length at 175, and apply the same fix in os/commands/grep.c to keep the builds identical.; Add a w_depth >= 8 guard in collect() before recursing so deep trees skip rather than overrun the fixed depth-8 tables.; Correct the misleading 'SYS_PUTC clobbers P1/P2' comment (line 353) and the cn 'stride 32' comment (lines 790-792) so docs match verified behavior and the fixed constant.


### `os/commands-asm/head.asm`  
**Code: B  ·  Docs: A**  ·  P8X ASSEMBLY

Hand-coded HEAD /BIN command: parses an optional -N count (default 10) and optional filename from the argument tail, opens the file or stdin via the shared stdin engine, and copies input to stdout until N newline-terminated lines have been emitted. Also handles -h/-H usage and file-not-found.

A genuinely solid, careful piece of hand assembly. Every JC/JNC branch after CMP/SUB is correct, multi-byte carry is chained properly in all three places that need it (arg increment, line counter, mul10n, digit accumulate), the pointer-clobber discipline (spill P2 to h_arg, reload after each call) is applied consistently, and JSR/RTS are balanced on every path. The main weaknesses are code duplication (the 4-instruction P2 reload appears six times) and a few wasted cycles rather than defects. Documentation is excellent and, unusually, accurate. I found no confirmed correctness bug.

**Findings:**

- _low_ · efficiency · less_hge (lines 215-219) — less re-executes LDA lines+1 / LDB n+1 / CMP immediately after the first CMP at lines 209-211. JC does not modify flags, so the Z flag from the first CMP is still live at less_hge; the whole re-compare is redundant. → Delete lines 216-218 and branch on the existing Z: after 'JC less_hge' the fall-through path handles lines_hi<n_hi, and less_hge can start directly with 'JNZ less_no' using the preserved Z flag.
- _low_ · efficiency · whole file — h_sk, h_dd, h_ds, h_d1, and the -N reload (repeated LDA h_arg/TAP2L/LDA h_arg+1/TAP2H) — The identical 4-instruction sequence that reloads P2 from h_arg appears six times, costing bytes in a command that must fit in the TPA. → Factor it into a leaf subroutine (e.g. 'h_ldp2: LDA h_arg / TAP2L / LDA h_arg+1 / TAP2H / RTS') and JSR it. Verify it doesn't clobber a value the caller needs in A (callers always reload A from (P2) afterward, so it is safe).
- _low_ · efficiency · mul10n (lines 174-205) — n*10 is computed by ten 16-bit additions of the saved multiplicand — the comment calls this 'obviously correct' and it is, but it is ~10x the work of a shift-add. → Optional: n*10 == (n<<3)+(n<<1) using SHL through carry for the 16-bit shifts. Only worth it if code size/speed matters here; the current version is clearer, so this is a deliberate-tradeoff note, not a defect.
- _low_ · correctness · h_open / openarg status check (lines 110-113) — Only status==2 (not found) is handled; any other nonzero error status from openarg falls through into the copy loop and is treated as a successful open. Unverified whether openarg can return other error codes. → Confirm openarg's full status contract in the stdin engine. If codes other than 0/2 are possible, branch them to an error message too, or document that only 0 and 2 are ever returned.
- _low_ · correctness · h_chk / -N parse (lines 41-68) — A bare '-' or '- ' (dash followed by no digits) silently yields n=0 (prints nothing) rather than being rejected or treated as stdin, and the default of 10 is discarded the moment '-' is seen. Unverified against head.c's intended behavior. → Cross-check os/commands/head.c: if a lone '-' should mean stdin or should keep the default count, add a guard so that zeroing n only happens once at least one digit has been consumed.
- _low_ · clarity · data section (lines 234-240) — Scratch cells mt/mcnt/mcar for mul10n and hch/h_arg/n/lines are all interleaved in one flat block; the mul10n-private scratch is indistinguishable from the command's live state. → Group and comment the mul10n-private scratch separately (already partially done) or prefix them consistently (they use 'm'/'h' prefixes but 'n','lines','mt' break the pattern). Minor readability only.

**Docs:** Strong file header explains the ABI contract (P2 = arg tail, syscalls/nextc clobber P1/P2, 16-bit LE counters), the openarg/nextc interface, and the load model. Each subroutine (h_ainc, mul10n, less) has a header naming its effect and clobbers. Inline comments are accurate against the code, including the non-obvious ones: the digit-range reasoning at lines 62-63, the "P2 unchanged in mem" note at line 45, and the carry-propagation comments. Minor gaps: openarg is documented as returning "2 == not found" but the code silently treats every other nonzero status as success (undocumented assumption); mul10n's comment doesn't note that 16-bit overflow is intentionally discarded; the meaning of the A=0 argument to SYS_PUTS (apparently "no trailing newline") is never stated.

**Strengths:** Every carry/borrow branch after CMP/SUB is correct — the common JC/JNC inversion bug class is absent throughout (digit range test, less, mul10n carry, both 16-bit increments).; Disciplined pointer handling: P2 is spilled to h_arg and reloaded after every call that can clobber it, and the INP2 peek at line 45 correctly leaves the in-memory pointer untouched, exactly as its comment claims.; 16-bit little-endian arithmetic is chained correctly in all four places (h_ainc, digit accumulate, line counter, mul10n) despite the ISA having no add-with-carry.; JSR/RTS are balanced on every exit path (h_done, h_nf, h_usage); no stack imbalance.; Documentation is thorough and, verified line-by-line, accurate — rare for hand assembly.

**Top improvements:** Factor the repeated 4-instruction 'reload P2 from h_arg' sequence into a single subroutine to reclaim TPA space.; Simplify less by reusing the Z flag from the first CMP instead of re-comparing the high bytes.; Confirm and document openarg's complete status contract so non-2 error codes aren't silently treated as a successful open.; Cross-check the bare-'-' / no-digit edge case against head.c to ensure n=0 vs default-10 vs stdin behavior is intended.


### `os/commands-asm/man.asm`  
**Code: B  ·  Docs: A**  ·  ASM (P8X)
